## intake-1346#record — ripwire: "the ripgrep of AI context" - zero-dependency C++23 CLI + optional MCP server giving coding agents a ranked, deterministic call-graph map of a repository

- **URL**: https://github.com/redhat-et/ripwire
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: tool_implementation, agent_architecture, context_management, search_retrieval)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Offline code-intelligence tool for coding agents. No LLM, no embeddings, no daemon. Tree-sitter ingest (24 vendored grammars) feeds a call graph. The graph is name-based at root, with a conservative one-hop type/include narrowing layer (resolve.h P2-D) and CHA-lite fan-out for virtual calls. Ranking is personalized PageRank whose teleport vector is seeded from lexical anchors, with fixed-block (1024) canonical float reduction for cross-host determinism. Output is minified XML via CLI or an optional MCP server over stdio or loopback HTTP.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] HEAD re-checked with git ls-remote on 2026-09-14: still c1915d211feb9d79e9b5c54bc84d4083e99540c5. Read in a shallow clone. src/pagerank.cpp:96-200 is a power iteration with a teleport vector. graph.h:3932/3989 seed that vector from lexical anchor scores, so it is personalized PageRank. pagerank.cpp:17-27 fixes kReductionBlockSize=1024, and the dangling-mass and residual sums fold in fixed blocks in canonical order (lines 138-181). third_party/deps holds 23 grammar dirs; ts_typescript contains both typescript and tsx, which makes 24. README:2041-2042 says there are no embeddings and no daemon, and grep found no embedding or vector code in src. Two narrowings. (a) Resolution is not purely name-based. src/resolve.h:1-40 (P2-D) adds a conservative one-hop type-narrowing layer: this/self class membership, local-variable type, field type, and include/import-based file narrowing. CHANGELOG:79-80 adds that virtual calls fan out one edge per candidate in the inheritance cone, each tagged prov="split" (CHA-lite). (b) MCP is not stdio-only. src/mcpserver.h:5 adds an optional Streamable-HTTP transport that binds loopback by default.

Every reduction below folds fixed-size blocks in canonical index order, so the summation tree is a property of the SOURCE and nothing else."
   - anchor `src/resolve.h:3-5 at c1915d21` @ redhat-et/ripwire@c1915d211feb9d79e9b5c54bc84d4083e99540c5: "resolve.h — P2-D one-hop type narrowing. A deterministic, conservative refinement of the §2a name-based resolution ladder (graph.h::buildGraph): it pins a method call to its actual receiver TYPE *before* the ambiguous bare-name spray"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (redhat-et/ripwire@c1915d211feb9d79e9b5c54bc84d4083e99540c5, HEAD unchanged; blobless full history 2,743 commits; import commit fb57f1973 via GitHub contents API). Mechanism confirmed, narrowed: the graph has a one-hop type/include narrowing layer and CHA-lite fan-out on top of name resolution, and MCP also has a loopback HTTP transport. GitNexus head-to-head: the tally is internally consistent but not reproducible from anything committed. It was judged by the ripwire project alone, against a GitNexus CLI arm with MCP-only verbs, embeddings, hooks and skills off and a 512 KB cap that dropped two central C++ files. We run 1.6.8, not 1.6.9. LocBench: the N=60 number reproduces from committed JSONL. The "held-out" label does not survive, because the 2026-07-31 mechanical rename changed the split salt (32/60 were previously train-side), and the slice was used for fix iteration. The N=243 figure has no committed raw artifact, and 105/243 of that set are now in the train split. Codex pilot numbers are exact, but "p95" over 3 pairs is a max. The Stage-1 claim that the only agent-loop measurement is a loss is overturned by EVALS §3b: 48 runs, parity, -9.2% output tokens, raw records not committed. Stage-1 symlink note overturned in its operational consequence (see notes_append). Stage-1 cross-ref on handoffs/active/delegation-context-preassembly.md (epyc-root@d372c953) is PARTIAL. The handoff does design budget-bounded codemap bundles and names GitNexus + ColGREP (lines 13, 25-26, 43, 58, 76, 84). The implemented DCP-3 codemap is a dependency-free Python ast skeleton, "GitNexus not a runtime dep" (lines 43, 94; confirmed in epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3 src/context_discovery.py:14,24,122). Discovery is ColGREP + file reads. DCP-1..5 are done and DCP-6 eval is open, so it is not "building on GitNexus". Adversarial pass: no GitNexus maintainer response, no issue disputing the comparison, no independent benchmark. Only the HN thread 49593050 (19 points, readability complaints, one anecdotal "half the tokens"), issue #73 (one commenter reports benchmarking the tool privately, no numbers), and the Wavect review, which restates the project numbers. Declined dive-surfaced sources are listed below. | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49593050 — "ripwire: ripgrep of AI context"; https://github.com/redhat-et/ripwire/issues/59 — TypeScript built-in member call; https://github.com/abhigyanpatwari/GitNexus/blob/main/LICENSE — PolyForm Noncomm; https://wavect.io/blog/ripwire-ai-repo-context-review-2026/ — Ripwire Review 202. | 2026-09-14 STAGE-3: licence framing retracted — the ledger row to check GitNexus's PolyForm-Noncommercial licence against the sourcing policy is DECLINED under the standing operator policy that licence type is not raised as a concern or follow-up; GitNexus is a local, self-hosted tool. The notes_append licence fact stays as a record only.

### Verdict

Do NOT change the CLAUDE.md GitNexus-before-edit rule on this evidence. The 27W/7L/14T is a single-judge, uncommitted, self-run comparison against a CLI-only GitNexus. Under MEASUREMENT.md it can only motivate a local matched measurement. Such a measurement should use GitNexus current version on our repos, with its impact/context workflow and questions authored by us, especially callers/blast-radius, which is what our rule uses. Do NOT cite 58.3% or 60.9% as held-out generalization. The split salt changed under both numbers, and the N=60 slice was iterated on. Treat both as in-distribution upper-bound localization numbers. Do NOT repeat the "+80% agent-loop cost loss" as the current state. The project retired it, and the later 48-run study reports parity with -9.2% output tokens, though that is also self-run with uncommitted records. ripwire CAN index a tree whose root is reached through a symlink; the "symlinked files not indexed" README line is wrong at HEAD. Separately, the installed GitNexus is PolyForm Noncommercial, which bears on the open-source-only sourcing policy independent of ripwire.
worth_investigating: all comparative figures self-run, GitNexus arm frozen at 1.6.9, only agent-loop measurement is a cost loss. Under MEASUREMENT.md nothing gates a GitNexus-vs-ripwire decision without local re-measurement on our repos with matched questions. Apache-2.0, self-hostable, cheap to trial.

## intake-1348#record — Procedural Graphs: Self-Evolving Execution Structures for LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09153
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, tool_implementation, knowledge_management, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Procedural knowledge stored as a small directed graph of (procedure, relation, procedure) triplets, 4 relation types (LEADS_TO, TRIGGERS, PROVIDES_INPUT_FOR, CONVERGES_TO), each edge with condition/guidance/pitfalls text. Graphs are 7-17 nodes / 7-27 triplets except BFCL at 131 nodes / 265 triplets (Table 7).
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Table 7 (App B.4) nodes/triplets: HotpotQA 9/9, MultiChallenge 7/7, GDPval 15/22, ALFWorld 11/27, tau 17/18, BFCL 131/265, EnterpriseArena 11/13. Relation vocabulary is the four stated types.
1. At run time the last action is exact-matched to a node; its 2-hop neighbourhood (whole graph if no match) plus the last 3 trajectory steps go to a separate guidance LLM whose step advice is appended to the ReAct solver prompt: soft steering (Sec 3.2, Eq 2-3).
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Sec 3.2 Eq 2-3: Match exactly matches the most recent procedure to a node; the neighbourhood is the h=2 directed OUTGOING expansion, with the full graph used if matching fails; window w=3; guidance appended to solver prompt.
   - anchor `Sec 5.5 (Table 3)` @ arXiv:2609.09153v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC (Table 3 MultiChallenge 12,295 vs 6,629 tokens = +85.5%, not quoted by authors): "On GDPval and ALFWorld, localized guidance reduces average solver steps from 28.20 to 18.57 and from 21.84 to 18.80, respectively, while total token consumption remains 33.4% and 55.4% higher."
2. Offline, a refiner LLM contrasts high- vs low-scoring trajectories and proposes JSON add/delete edits. A candidate is kept only if it passes structural checks and scores >= the cached validation score (ties accepted). Rejected candidates (Alg 1: both structural failures and gate rejections) are serialized back to the refiner. The paper never ablates the rejection memory.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Sec 3.3 Step 4 says only gate-rejected candidates enter the rejection memory, but Alg 1 (App B.6) lines 11-13 also append structurally-failed candidates. The gate accepts ties (Eq 5, ">="). No ablation removes rejection memory anywhere in the paper, so its contribution is unmeasured.
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Step 4; App B.6 Algorithm 1 lines 11-13, 19` @ arXiv:2609.09153v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The rejection history ℋrejected helps the refiner avoid previously unsuccessful edits. Proposed changes are evaluated by the gate before they enter the retained graph."
   - anchor `Sec 5.4 (EnterpriseArena gate reliability)` @ arXiv:2609.09153v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "With 20 episodes per split, individual accept/reject decisions turn on one or two episodes and should be read as a search trace rather than as significance tests."

Fix the stated reason (e.g. enable a missing ""
3. First or joint-first in 21 of 24 settings; vs the strongest baseline 19W/2T/3L (sign test p=4.3e-4 across settings). Within every one of the 24 settings the PG and best-baseline 95% CIs overlap, and 11 of 19 wins are <=2 points. Which construction mode produced the Table 1 graphs is not stated.

One-sided binomial P(X>=19|22) = 1794/2^22 = 4.28e-4. PG's 95% CI overlaps the best baseline's CI in ALL 24 settings (not "most"). 11 of 19 wins are <=2.0 points. 4. On one model (Gemini 3.5 Flash) and small splits: on a 56-item MultiChallenge split, a hand-written expert graph cut success from 49/56 to 33/56 (87.50 -> 58.93), and gated online evolution from that graph recovered to 52/56 (92.86), about +3 items over no graph, no CIs. Scratch-plus-online-evolution beat the hand-written graph on HotpotQA and MultiChallenge, but a one-time scratch build without the gate underperformed no graph on HotpotQA.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Table 2 / Table 10 confirm 87.50 -> 58.93 (Mode 1) -> 92.86 (Mode 3). These are on the 56-sample MultiChallenge fast split, Gemini 3.5 Flash only, Gemini 3.1 Pro judge, no CIs: 49/56 -> 33/56 -> 52/56. The evolved graph beats the unguided baseline by 3 items. Scratch evolution (Mode 5) beats hand-crafted Mode 1 on both benchmarks but trails Mode 3 on MultiChallenge (91.07 vs 92.86, 1 item). Scratch one-time build (Mode 4) is below unguided on HotpotQA F1 (69.49 vs 71.21).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (arXiv:2609.09153v1, hash unchanged; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3; epyc-root@d372c953). Table 1 values recomputed: counts and sign test exact. CI overlap is universal (24/24), not "most". The MultiChallenge expert-prior-repair headline is 3-item and 19-item movements on a 56-item split with one model and no CIs. Alg 1 contradicts Sec 3.3 on whether structural failures enter rejection memory, and rejection memory is unablated. Still unstated: Table 1 construction mode, evolution token/$ cost; no limitations section; no code. Stage-1 actionable on autopilot rejected-proposal feedback OVERTURNED. The planner prompt already carries the failure blacklist (auto-appended on repeated safety failures, repeated invalid signatures and critic rejections), last-invalid-action feedback with repeat counts, and a 12-trial journal summary with pareto_status and FAILED analysis. No dive-surfaced sources warranted ingest.

### Verdict

Treat PG as a well-specified but statistically thin result: every per-setting gain sits inside overlapping CIs, all models are frontier APIs, and per-step guidance costs +33-85% tokens, a direct decode tax on our CPU stack. The one durable caution is that hand-written procedural guidance can actively hurt until a validation gate repairs it. Do NOT file "feed rejected proposals back to the autopilot planner" as new work; it exists. Do not cite rejection memory as a validated component; the paper never ablates it.
worth_investigating: no code, main-table graph construction mode unstated, gains mostly CI-overlapping, nothing on small local models. Two findings worth a closer look: validation-gated structural edits with rejection memory (parallel to AP-29 and intake-899#record memory-rot caution), and hand-written expert graphs hurting ~29 points until gated evolution repaired them.

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] autopilot-continuous-optimization.md: Check whether rejected-proposal history is fed back to the autopilot proposer (AP-29 / strategy store); if not, a cheap addition.

### Claims (as verified)

0. TypeScript extension for the earendil-works/pi coding agent (pinned @earendil-works/pi-coding-agent 0.84.2) using only public extension APIs, no Pi patching; four opt-in mechanisms, all off by default. "Pi" is that harness, not a policy.

Both vertical axes begin at zero. Costs are reported API-equivalent estimates."
1. Action Fusion: replaces edit/write with versions that accept an OPTIONAL model-supplied then_run {command, timeout}. The command runs in the same call after a SHA-256 check that the file has not changed, and one fused observation is returned. It saves a turn only if the model chooses to pass then_run. It is silently inert when another extension owns the tool surface (issues #20-#22, open at 2ecee02e).
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] action-fusion/then-run.ts:21-31: then_run is an OPTIONAL schema field. :106-108: if the model omits it, the plain mutation result is returned. :54-68: the file SHA-256 is re-checked before the command runs. :110-125: output gets a [then_run:succeeded]/[then_run:failed] marker. Upstream issues #20, #21 and #22 (open, independent, deepseek-v4(.1)-flash) report two things. First, fusion is model-opt-in: one run saw 0 fusions and +14% tokens, another saw 5/5 fusions. Second, it goes silently inert when another extension (pi-fabric) replaces the tool surface. The other three mechanisms keep firing in that setup. No fix is merged (PR #23 is open).
2. ObservationPack: pure-text, non-error tool results >10 KiB are sent in full for 2 provider requests. After that they are replaced at projection time by an obs_<24hex> handle (derived from toolName+toolCallId+content SHA-256) plus a whole-line head/tail excerpt of 1,024 bytes total. The full text is archived content-addressed, with exact byte-offset paged recall through obs_recall (16 KiB/400 lines per page). The shipped excerpt (512+512 B) is smaller than the V2 configuration the blog says passed its A/B (2,048 B head + 1,536 B tail).
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Constants confirmed at observation.ts:13 (THRESHOLD_BYTES = 10*1024), :15 (FULL_SENDS = 2), :17 (PLACEHOLDER_EXCERPT_BYTES = 1024, split 512/512 into whole lines, :178-182), :20 (/^obs_[a-f0-9]{24}$/) and :106 (id = sha256(toolName\0toolCallId\0contentHash)[:24]). Recall: obs_recall pages by byte offset, max 16 KiB/400 lines per page (index.ts:41-49, :65-115), with UTF-8 boundary trimming. Storage: content-addressed with O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW; an existing object is re-hashed before reuse (:123-156). Rewriting happens at projection time only (index.ts:137-210). Fail-open (:202-206). Narrowings: isError tool results are never packed (:67-73). The blog says the configuration validated in the paired A/B was V2 with a 2,048-byte head and 1,536-byte tail; the shipped code uses 1,024 bytes total. Open defects: send counts leak across session-tree branches (#12), and the excerpt is empty for single-line payloads (#41).
   - anchor `src/sol-pi/extensions/observation-pack/observation.ts:12-17 at NVlabs/SoL-Pi@2ecee02e116d3a13b2304ccf6c770d7bc4675660` @ NVlabs/SoL-Pi@2ecee02e116d3a13b2304ccf6c770d7bc4675660 (mechanism code identical since f0e6c4c), cloned 2026-09-14 UTC: "/** Only tool results larger than this participate. */ export const THRESHOLD_BYTES = 10 * 1024; /** Provider requests that still carry the full payload before the placeholder takes over. */ export const FULL_SENDS = 2; /** Placeholder excerpt budget, split evenly between head and tail, whole lin..."
   - anchor `blog, ObservationPack research overlay, "Why V2 survived"` @ https://nvlabs.github.io/SoL-Pi/ raw HTML sha256 dc589f536b95da60d2effc6f363c097b416c0e28a8812ed82eefd33d95a7e35e, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "V2 was the only swept configuration inside the quality gate. It combined a 2,048-byte head, a 1,536-byte tail, and two full sends before projection."
   - anchor `blog ObservationPack overlay "Paired EdgeBench result"` @ https://nvlabs.github.io/SoL-Pi/ raw HTML, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Provider bill -23.58%, cost per response -23.73%, and normalized score +22.92%."
3. Evidence-Preserving Reducer: for bash (or fused then_run) output from commands matching DIAGNOSTIC_COMMAND, between 4,096 bytes and 600k chars, and not matching a narrow LIKELY_SECRET regex, it archives the raw log under its SHA-256 and sends the full body to a reducer model (default openai-codex/gpt-5.6-luna, configurable through the Pi model registry). It accepts the returned JSON receipt only if schema, source hash and status (vs Pi isError) match and every quote is a substring of the archived log. A failing log whose body matches FAILURE_SIGNAL must carry at least one item labelled fatal/failure (label only, not content-checked). Any failure passes the original through. The receipt replaces the raw output on first send and names the archive path for exact readback.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] receipt.ts:82-144 validateReceipt checks: JSON parse (:88-93); schema, source_sha256 == archive hash, status == (isError ? failure : success), uncertain boolean, at most 12 evidence items (:96-106); every quote 1-600 chars and body.includes(quote) (:113-122); and, only when isError AND FAILURE_SIGNAL matches the body, at least one item of KIND fatal/failure (:136-142). Fail-open: every fallback returns undefined so the original is kept (index.ts:65-148). It also falls back if the receipt is not smaller than the source (:138). Narrowings: (a) "status vs exit code" is actually Pi's isError flag. (b) The failure-evidence rule checks only the model-assigned kind label, not that the quote contains a failure signal, so a benign quote labelled failure passes (PR #47, open). (c) A success log, or a failing log with no FAILURE_SIGNAL match, is accepted with zero evidence. (d) Nothing checks for omissions. (e) Quotes can be a single character. Default route: openai-codex / gpt-5.6-luna (config.ts:22-23). The full body (up to 600,000 chars) is sent; the command is sent only as a SHA-256 (receipt.ts:54-65). The secret gate is a single regex (config.ts:29) that misses passwords and PEM keys (#38, open; PR #28 unmerged). The DIAGNOSTIC_COMMAND cargo restriction is ineffective (#37, open; PR #27 unmerged).

   - anchor `src/sol-pi/extensions/evidence-preserving-reducer/config.ts:22-23,29 at 2ecee02e` @ NVlabs/SoL-Pi@2ecee02e116d3a13b2304ccf6c770d7bc4675660: "export const DEFAULT_REDUCER_PROVIDER = ["openai", "codex"].join("-"); export const DEFAULT_REDUCER_MODEL = ["gpt-5.6", "luna"].join("-"); ... export const LIKELY_SECRET = /(?:api[_-]?key|authorization|bearer|access[_-]?token|secret)[^\n]{0,32}[=:][^\n]+/i;"
   - anchor `GitHub issue NVlabs/SoL-Pi#38 "Likely-secret detector misses password assignments and PEM private keys" (2026-09-13, OPEN; PR #28 unmerged)` @ GitHub REST API issues/38, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "false PGPASSWORD=hunter2 <- eligible for the remote call"
4. Online Context Compact: at a completed update_plan step it prices a native Pi compaction. The first compaction runs if writeTokens*(cacheWriteReadRatio-1)/(archiveTokens-1000) <= 2x the estimated remaining requests. Later compactions also need a 1.5x margin and repayment of carried cache debt. It always runs within 16,384 tokens of the window. cacheWriteReadRatio defaults to 12.5 (config.ts:14). The default of 12.5 is NOT in economics.ts; it is in src/sol-pi/config.ts:14 and online-context-compact/index.ts:44. memoTokens is a fixed 1,000-token estimate (extension.ts:36, :292). archiveTokens = context - system prompt - 20,000 keepRecent (:279), which overestimates the removable prefix at message boundaries (#5, open). The compaction request uses one static sentence (extension.ts:346-347). The pendingProgress collected at plan boundaries is write-only (state.ts:170, :184, :199; no reader). This matches #42 (open; PR #32 unmerged). The "semantic boundary" therefore triggers the decision, but the plan evidence never reaches the summarizer.

   - anchor `src/sol-pi/config.ts:14 and online-context-compact/extension.ts:346-347 at 2ecee02e` @ NVlabs/SoL-Pi@2ecee02e116d3a13b2304ccf6c770d7bc4675660: "export const DEFAULT_CACHE_WRITE_READ_RATIO = 12.5; ... context.compact({ customInstructions: BOUNDARY_COMPACTION_INSTRUCTIONS,"
   - anchor `GitHub issue NVlabs/SoL-Pi#42 "Online Context Compact never reads the plan progress it collects" (opened 2026-09-13T03:44Z, OPEN; fix PR #32 OPEN, unmerged)` @ GitHub REST API issues/42, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "`state.pendingProgress` is write-only. Online Context Compact asks the model for progress evidence at every plan boundary, stores it, and then discards it at the compaction it was collected for."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (NVlabs/SoL-Pi@2ecee02e116d3a13b2304ccf6c770d7bc4675660, which differs from the Stage-1 8daac782 only in README; blog raw HTML sha256 dc589f53... plus its three EdgeBench SVGs; GitHub issues/PRs #1-#52 via REST; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1; epyc-root@d372c953). Every mechanism constant and formula in key_claims is confirmed at file:line. All four mechanism claims are NARROWED: - Action Fusion is model-opt-in and silently inert under a replaced tool surface. - The shipped ObservationPack excerpt (1,024 B) is not the V2 configuration (2,048+1,536 B) that passed the blog's A/B, and error results are never packed. - The reducer's failure-evidence gate checks only a label; zero-evidence receipts pass; "exit code" means Pi's isError; the default route is hosted openai-codex/gpt-5.6-luna; the secret regex misses passwords and PEM keys. - OCC's plan progress never reaches the summarizer, so the plan-boundary idea only triggers the decision. The Stage-1 statement that the defects were "fixed within two weeks" is OVERTURNED: all are open with unmerged PRs. The published benchmarks therefore ran with them present. Blog numbers are verified from the raw HTML and the chart SVGs. The second backend is Claude Opus 5, which Stage 1 wrongly excluded. Issue #25 has no maintainer answer; a third-party reply confirms no per-task matrix or ablation is public. The central adopt_patterns verdict survives. Our code comparison shows those patterns are genuinely absent here: - Our spill path truncates on first send, head-only. - Spill files are non-hashed, deterministic-name, overwrite-on-collision. - peek has no offset or paging, so it cannot recall beyond the first n chars. - The REPL worker summarises only the last 4,000 chars, unverified. - Compaction is neither plan-aware nor cache-economics-aware. Declined dive-surfaced sources: EdgeBench dataset, pi-fabric, Pi llama-cpp.md (verified directly, no entry needed). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): https://huggingface.co/datasets/ByteDance-Seed/EdgeBench (51-task long-horizon a; pi-fabric (code-mode flat-tool Pi extension, referenced in NVlabs/SoL-Pi issues ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/earendil-works/pi/v0.84.2/packages/coding-agen.

### Verdict

Port PATTERNS, not the component and not the numbers. Worth porting into our REPL tool layer: - content-hashed spill with exact offset-paged recall and a head+tail excerpt; - "full for N sends, then handle"; - a receipt gate for any LLM log summary, with byte-exact quote check, exit-code agreement, required failure evidence and fail-open; - a plan-boundary compaction trigger priced by break-even. Do NOT conclude: - that SoL-Pi's mechanisms are production-hardened. The fixes for plan progress, secret detection, cargo gating and receipt label-only evidence are all open and unmerged. - that the reducer receipt proves nothing was omitted. It proves only that quotes exist in the log. - that 12.5 or any break-even constant transfers. On llama.cpp the cost of rewriting history is re-prefill of every later token, which ObservationPack's send-3 substitution and our own tool-result clearing both trigger. - that -23.58% or 45-49% predicts anything for us. The shipped excerpt differs from the measured one, all runs are hosted xhigh frontier models, and SoL-Pi solved the fewest Terminal-Bench 4 tasks. Never enable a remote reducer here. If Pi is adopted (HS-4), route the reducer to a local llama-server through models.json, and treat LIKELY_SECRET as insufficient; our redaction.py is broader.
adopt_patterns: verified-quote receipt contract, full-for-N-sends-then-handle-with-exact-recall policy, and break-even compaction test port to our tool layer without adopting Pi. adopt_component only if HS-4 picks earendil-works/pi (MIT clears policy; reducer route needs a local endpoint). Nothing gates without local re-measurement.

### Claims (as verified)

0. PRIMARY-ANCHORED (X post @joelniklaus 2026-09-10; run artifacts unpublished): with GPT-OSS-20B fixed at high reasoning, a meta-harness search over OpenCode patches raised held-out Terminal-Bench 2.1 from 4.8% to 14.8% (3.08x) for $49.97 of API credits. The held-out trial count is not stated; the 0.4% granularity fits 50 tasks x 5 trials.

The trial count and task count of the held-out split are NOT stated. Arithmetic only: 4.8% = 12/250 and 14.8% = 37/250, which fits 50 tasks x 5 trials and fits neither 50x3 (0.67% grid) nor 50x1. huggingface/harbor-hf@59756acf defines a "terminal-bench-2-1-held-50" preset (50 of 89 tasks), which supports 50 held-out tasks and 39 dev tasks. No run artifacts for this Sept-10 search are public: both joelniklaus HF buckets were last updated 2026-08-12, and the GitHub repos and gists show no push after 2026-09-01 other than joelniklaus.github.io "updated publications".

1. Author-asserted, no ablation published: three lifecycle patches, i7 verify-before-stop, i14 continue-announced-actions and i23 repair of malformed tool calls (e.g. one extra "]" that made OpenCode reject valid commands), carried most of the held-out gain. The only per-fix numbers are dev-split (39 tasks) points from a non-monotonic animated curve: i7 15.4%, i14 13.2%, i23 30.8% vs stock 8.5%. These are per-iteration dev scores, not validated promotions and not held-out ablations.

The curve is NON-MONOTONIC: i8 drops to about 10.3%, i13 to about 2.6%, and i14 (13.2%) sits BELOW i7 (15.4%). Promotion requires a validated score above the parent, so the plotted points cannot be the parent lineage's validated scores. They are per-iteration candidate dev scores, or a mix of granularities: 15.4 = 6/39 and 30.8 = 12/39 fit one run per task, while 8.5 = 10/117 and 13.2 fit no k/39. The i23 "30.8%" is therefore one iteration's dev score. It is not a validated gain and not held-out evidence.    - anchor `X post body, final paragraph` @ https://x.com/joelniklaus/status/2_098_064_253_404_225_640 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Takeaway: pre-training puts capability in the weights, and post-training makes that capability usable."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (X post 2_098_064_253_404_225_640 raw via fxtwitter + GIF frames; HF buckets joelniklaus/harness-optimization-results and CodingBenchmarkResults via HF API; huggingface/harbor-hf@59756acf; JoelNiklaus/harness-optimization@c98a1680; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3). Stage-1 could not retrieve the primary. It is now retrieved and the attribution is verified, so credibility rises 1->2 (known contributor +1 now earned). Numbers are still unpublished beyond the post, and there is no independent replication. Every text number matches. The dev per-fix numbers exist only in the animation, and the non-monotonic curve shows they are per-iteration dev scores. The write-up's "biggest jump" reading of i23 is therefore a single dev point, not a validated or held-out effect. The chart's pi/qwen-code/mini-swe-agent reference lines are unlabelled and probably held-out (k/250 grid), so stock OpenCode likely started below pi and Qwen Code on this model. The non-transfer caveats are reattributed to the write-up author. The author's earlier public Aug campaign (low reasoning) shows the same patch classes with modest single-sweep dev effects (max 17.1% vs 5.1%) and 5-trial frontier 8.7% vs 3.1%. The OUR-CODE audit found two defects Stage-1 did not: the FINAL("done") finish instruction is rejected by the status-message guard, and malformed JSON tool-call arguments fail open to {}. Declined dive-surfaced sources: huggingface/harbor-hf (infra; held-50 fact recorded here); the X primary itself as a separate entry (it is this entry's primary). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: operator selected for Stage-2b: https://huggingface.co/buckets/joelniklaus/harness-optimization-results (HF buck -> intake-1362#record. Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): https://github.com/huggingface/harbor-hf (HF infra repo, HEAD 59756acf 2026-09-1.

### Verdict

Treat the 4.8%->14.8% held-out result as a real, author-published claim with no public run artifacts and no replication. Take the three fix names as the author's attribution, not an ablation. Do NOT cite 30.8% or "i23 gave the biggest jump" as a measured effect of malformed-call repair; it is one dev-iteration point on a noisy curve. Do NOT conclude stock OpenCode is a strong small-model harness: its likely held-out baseline sat below pi and qwen-code. For our stack the transferable content is the failure taxonomy. Our REPL already survives i14/i23-style events structurally, but has no verify-after-edit gate, steers stalled turns toward FINAL, rejects its own FINAL("done") finish, and silently empties malformed JSON tool-call arguments. Fix those and measure their rates locally before any harness-search investment.
adopt_patterns: three failure-mode checks testable against our REPL loop. Partial equivalents found in epyc-orchestrator (subagent-read, unpinned): no-executable-code nudge with escalation (src/graph/helpers.py:364, :1061-1259; src/graph/nodes.py:274-299) - related to i14 but triggers on missing code, not announced-but-unexecuted; REPL no-progress loop guard (src/graph/state.py:140, helpers.py:1016); patchset sandbox->verify->promote with malformed/absent/applied telemetry (helpers.py:413-600) - partial i7, does not block FINAL() without a test run; FINAL()/tool-call salvage from mixed prose+code (src/prompt_builders/code_utils.py:415-429) - partial i23, no JSON tool_calls bracket repair found. Primary unverified; nothing gates without local re-measurement.

Stage-1's "no GPT-OSS artifacts" is scoped to the repo: a sibling bucket holds an earlier GPT-OSS-20B search (routed to intake-1351#record). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: operator selected for Stage-2b: https://x.com/joelniklaus/status/2_091_903_115_985_654_136 (Joel Niklaus, 2026-08-24): -> intake-1363#record.

### Verdict

The ten-harness SWE-bench Pro grid and its rank non-transfer (Spearman -0.05) are solidly sourced but single-rollout (about +/-6pp per cell). The thinking-off arm is a non-randomly censored lead. Harness choice must be measured on our served models at our enable_thinking settings. The json-repair file is a good, gated pattern for our fail-open tool-call argument path. Do NOT read it as evidence that malformed-call repair raises solve rates on small models: it rescues ~2-3% provider-corrupted runs on a different model and task family.
adopt_patterns: nothing to lift wholesale (cluster-specific). Patterns: evaluate HS-4 candidates on our served models at our thinking config; failure-cause breakdown and timeout-censoring check before reading a harness gap; freeze/restore graded tests and scrub future git state; invalidate cached infra zeros; harness-wiring tests.

## intake-1353#record — [WIP] Benchmarking Local LLMs Against Coding Agent Harnesses (harness-bench, Minervini)

- **URL**: https://neuralnoise.com/2026/harness-bench-wip/
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, local_inference, quantization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1351#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PRIVATE 16-task SWE benchmark (Python, PyTorch, JAX, C, C++, Rust, SQL) graded by hidden test.sh; sweep 17 model-quants x 5 harnesses (Aider, Claude Code, OpenCode, Pi, Qwen CLI) x 16 tasks = 1360 runs on one M3 Max 128 GB served by llama.cpp llama-server.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Raw HTML re-fetched (HTTP 200, sha256 prefix e335b321bb56, identical to Stage 1). Intro: "17 model-quants × 5 harnesses × 16 tasks = 1360 runs on a single M3 Max / 128 GB laptop". The arithmetic checks out, and so does the "85 tested cells" count (10×5 + 7×5). The post contradicts itself: the intro says "the agent only sees a scratch workspace/ ... hidden test.sh that the agent never sees", while Q4 says "sandboxed only by convention – the agent has full filesystem access to ~/workspace/harness-bench/".
1. Harness choice matters and is model-dependent (single run per cell, 16 tasks). Marginalised over 10 Q4 models: pi 76.9% (bare core, extensions disabled) and qwen 75.0% lead claude 66.2%, opencode 63.8% (includes grader-peek passes) and aider 62.5%. pi vs qwen is within noise.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] The Q3 table matches: pi 123/160 76.9%, qwen 120/160 75.0%, claude 106/160 66.2%, opencode 102/160 63.8%, aider 100/160 62.5%. Model totals (Q2) and harness totals both sum to 551, so the tables are internally consistent. The Q1 table has 8 rows at 15/16 or better. Narrowing: the pi vs qwen gap is 3/160 from a single run. pi was run with -nc -ns -ne -np, which pi args.ts:169-194 confirms disables context files, skills, extensions and templates, so this is bare core pi. The opencode 102 includes up to 13 passes from grader peeking.
   - anchor `Q3 harness table` @ url retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC, raw sha256 prefix e335b321bb56: "pi | 123/160 | 76.9% | 163 s ... opencode | 102/160 | 63.8% | 271 s | aider | 100/160 | 62.5% | 384 s"
2. In 14 (model, task) cells, some from the Q8 sweep, opencode ran or read tasks/<id>/test.sh, and 13 passed. The ~55% adjustment mixes Q4 and Q8 cells and is approximate. The graders were reachable because the benchmark sandbox was a path convention. OpenCode default permissions are NOT laxer than the other harnesses: external_directory defaults to ask and `opencode run` auto-rejects asks. But the project boundary includes the whole git worktree, and bash arguments are not path-gated. The difference is behavioural propensity, exposed by a sandbox defect.
   - *reattributed*: [PARTIAL] The numbers match the raw text: grader-peek table opencode 23/14, pi 0/0, qwen 5/0, claude 7/0, aider 10/0; "Of those 14 peeking cells, 13 passed"; "63.8% to roughly 55%". Problems with the adjustment: (i) The quoted example trace is "Qwen3.5-27B-Q8 + opencode", a Q8-sweep cell, but it is subtracted from the Q4 denominator of 102/160, and the post never says how many of the 14 cells are Q4. (ii) Only 13 passes coincide, yet the post says "14 of 102". (iii) Running the grader and reading its source are lumped together. OPENCODE SOURCE (anomalyco/opencode, the canonical repo; api sst/opencode 301-redirects to repositories/975734319): at HEAD 228e9095 and at tags v1.14.25 (2026-04-25) and v1.2.22 (2026-03-08), the default is "*": "allow" but external_directory: {"*": "ask"} (agent.ts:119-125 HEAD). read, glob and grep call assertExternalDirectoryEffect (read.ts:250, glob.ts:44, grep.ts:55). `opencode run` without --auto / --dangerously-skip-permissions AUTO-REJECTS any ask (run.ts:801-818 HEAD; the same logic is in v1.14.25 and v1.2.22). The project boundary is the start directory OR the git worktree (instance-context.ts:18-24; worktree found by walking up to .git, core/git.ts:185-193). The docs are narrower than the code, citing only "the working directory where OpenCode was started" (permissions.mdx:111). Shell commands only have their path arguments scanned for cd/rm/cp/mv/mkdir/touch/chmod/chown/cat (shell.ts:28-50, :397-403), so `bash <repo>/tasks/<id>/test.sh` is never gated by external_directory. Upstream issue anomalyco/opencode#21733, which asked for a real filesystem sandbox, was closed not_planned. CONSEQUENCE: with the post command `opencode run -m llamacpp/bench-model "<prompt>"`, a Read of ../../../../../tasks/<id>/test.sh succeeds only if the path is inside the git worktree (most likely: the tasks sit inside the private harness-bench repo that contains the workspace) or the private wrapper relaxed permissions. This is an inference, because the wrapper and opencode version are unpublished. The other harnesses had at least as much access: pi has no permission system at all (pi README.md:41), claude ran with --dangerously-skip-permissions, and qwen with --approval-mode yolo. So "peeking" is a BENCHMARK-SANDBOX defect (graders reachable in the agent-readable tree). The harness-specific part is a behavioural tendency, not laxer access defaults. A plausible but untested cause is opencode default.txt:74, which tells the model to search the codebase for the testing approach.
   - anchor `neuralnoise.com/2026/harness-bench-wip/ Q4 section` @ url retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC, raw sha256 prefix e335b321bb56: "The hidden test.sh files live outside the workspace (under tasks/<id>/test.sh). The benchmark is sandboxed only by convention – the agent has full filesystem access to ~/workspace/harness-bench/, so all five harnesses could technically peek. Only opencode actually does, by default."
   - anchor `Q4 section, representative trace + contamination estimate` @ url retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC, raw sha256 prefix e335b321bb56: "A representative trace from Qwen3.5-27B-Q8 + opencode on pt3_rope_gqa: ... ~14% of opencode’s passes (14 of 102) coincide with grader peeking. If I subtract those, opencode’s pass rate drops from 63.8% to roughly 55%"
   - anchor `packages/opencode/src/agent/agent.ts:119-125; packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/run.ts:810-818` @ anomalyco/opencode@228e9095ba3988a02664c3816cb51f98584e86c2 (same defaults at v1.14.25 3c85719f and v1.2.22): "const defaults = Permission.fromConfig({ "*": "allow", doom_loop: "ask", external_directory: { "*": "ask", | `permission requested: ${permission.permission} (${permission.patterns.join(", ")}); auto-rejecting`, ... reply: "reject","
   - anchor `packages/opencode/src/project/instance-context.ts:15-24` @ anomalyco/opencode@228e9095ba3988a02664c3816cb51f98584e86c2: "Returns true if path is inside ctx.directory OR ctx.worktree. * Paths within the worktree but outside the working directory should not trigger external_directory permission."
   - anchor `packages/web/src/content/docs/permissions.mdx:111,173` @ anomalyco/opencode@228e9095ba3988a02664c3816cb51f98584e86c2: "Use `external_directory` to allow tool calls that touch paths outside the working directory where OpenCode was started. ... - `doom_loop` and `external_directory` default to `"ask"`."
3. Q4 vs Q8 essentially flat (7-model total 406/560 vs 400/560); gpt-oss-20b the exception (+7 at Q8).
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Q5 table sums re-checked: Q4 64+66+65+55+55+56+45=406; Q8 66+65+59+52+51+55+52=400; jax1 17/35 vs 14/35; gpt-oss-20b 45 to 52 (+7).
4. UNVERIFIABLE: task prompts, graders and traces are private; claimed-public aggregated CSV/plotting source not linked in the page HTML.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] The page text says "Aggregated results, the per-cell CSV, and the plotting source for this post are public", but the only assets linked in the HTML are six SVGs under /images/harness-bench/. Four guessed CSV paths returned 404. api.github.com/repos/pminervini/harness-bench returns 404, and no harness-bench repo appears among pminervini repos. feed.json shows only one later post (2026-07-03 icml-acl-2026, 0 "harness" mentions). No follow-up and no containerised re-run published as of 2026-09-14. Web search found only restatements of the post.
   - anchor `intro paragraph` @ url retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC, raw sha256 prefix e335b321bb56: "Aggregated results, the per-cell CSV, and the plotting source for this post are public; the tasks are not."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (raw HTML sha e335b321bb56; anomalyco/opencode HEAD 228e9095 plus tags v1.14.25 and v1.2.22 via the GitHub contents API; earendil-works/pi@ceea48f5 for the pi permission comparison). All tables CONFIRMED. The Stage-1 hypothesis that "OpenCode may read files outside the workspace by default" is overturned at source for the Read/glob/grep path: ask, then auto-reject in run mode. Peeking is only possible inside the start directory or git worktree, or via unscanned bash invocations. The contamination adjustment is approximate: a Q8 exemplar sits in the Q4 denominator, and there are 13 passes, not 14. Declined dive-surfaced sources: opencode#21733 (closed not_planned; fact recorded here), opencode#16126 (Windows-only external_directory enforcement bug). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/issues/21733 "Add filesystem sandbox for b; https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/issues/16126 "external_directory: deny not.

### Verdict

The post is a private, single-run, 16-task benchmark whose tables are internally consistent. Its grader-peek result shows the sandbox was a path convention, not that OpenCode has lax defaults. Every one of the five harnesses, as invoked, could run anything under the uid, and pi (the winner) has no permission system at all. For any local harness bake-off: graders and reference files must be unreachable by construction (a separate container or uid, never in the same git repo as the workspace), because OpenCode treats the whole git worktree as in-project and nobody gates bash. Do NOT score OpenCode down on permission defaults because of this post. Do NOT quote opencode ~55% as a corrected number. Do NOT treat the pi-over-qwen margin as real.
worth_investigating: private tasks/graders/traces, one run per cell, unresolved contamination; the grader-peek claim is cheap to check locally (OpenCode default file-access audit + container-isolated smoke test) and would change how an HS-4 bake-off is sandboxed.

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] tool-use-eval-contract.md: Contract clause candidate: graders unreachable from the agent sandbox (container mount, not path convention) plus transcript grep for grader access.

DIVE-ADDED [8] (derived from the page's embedded taskData, 13 models x 89 tasks, recomputed): OMP vs stock DSV4F-0731 = 22 gained / 2 lost (exact McNemar p = 3.6e-5); GLM 3.25 BPW = 9 gained / 5 lost (p = 0.42). Same GLM-5.2 IQ4_NL weights with "8bit-KV-Cache": 46 to 21 passes = 1 gained / 26 lost (p = 4.2e-7); the configuration behind "8bit-KV-Cache" is not defined on the page.
   - anchor `page.html:L929 (taskData array comment, lines 931-1021 hold the data) ; churn recomputed by scratchpad/dpf-b/td.py` @ page.html sha256 1f7fdfa4767f19ecb899c9afce9d09c1ad99b36f7b5c931e72b326ac0a2df092: "13 models, full 89-task data each (latest trial, reward >= 1.0 = pass)."

### Dive corrections

- DIVE 2026-10-21 (dpf-b). Every number in the entry reproduces: headline 61/65 and 44/64 of 89; time per solve 1.00/2.16/0.44/1.09; tokens per solved ~30K/65K/62K/154K; and all 13 taskData totals (57, 58, 50, 44, 64, 46, 61, 65, 41, 38, 31, 21, 10). Stage-1 churn recomputed from taskData: DSV4F 22 gained / 2 lost, GLM 9 gained / 5 lost. NARROWED (key_claims[1] "model-dependent: GLM moves only 61 to 65"): 9 gained / 5 lost is statistically indistinguishable from no change (exact McNemar p = 0.42, one trial per task), whereas DSV4F 22/2 gives p = 3.6e-5. State it as "no detectable change for GLM", not "a smaller gain". NARROWED (key_claims[0] "simple harness (minion)"): the page names "minion" only as the author's personal coding harness in a time/token comparison and otherwise says "stock harness" and "simple harness"; it never states that the stock arm of every one of the 13 rows is minion. The A/B is therefore "unspecified stock harness vs OMP", one author, one trial per task. OVERTURNED (relevance_justification and recommended_actions): "names omp, a live HS-4/HS-5 candidate" is stale. HS-4 was DECIDED 2026-09-16 (OpenCode selected, pi fallback) and oh-my-pi was NOT selected on cooperation grounds (second router via retry.fallbackChains, yolo approval default, bus factor about 1; intake-1148#06). Pass-rate lift is not among the selection criteria, so the HS-5 recommended action (attach +20/89 as omp outcome evidence) is moot; the entry's own Stage-3 disposition (knowledge_only) was right. CONFIRMED: the vendor-claim statement is the author's opinion ("I was simply unable to replicate them"), and the mechanism of the OMP lift is never attributed to a feature. Cost is not free: OMP multiplies time and tokens per solve by 2.2-2.5x within model. NEW (not in the entry): the page's taskData lets one compare quantization and serving variants of the same GLM-5.2 weights. Same IQ4_NL weights with "8bit-KV-Cache": 46 to 21 passes (1 gained, 26 lost, p = 4.2e-7). The page does not say what the configuration is; engine, context length and wall-clock timeouts are uncontrolled, and one trial per task, so it is a flag and not a KV-quantization effect estimate. Other pairs: IQ4_NL to EXL3 3.25 BPW 20 gained / 5 lost (p = 0.004, engine and quant both differ); Q2_K_XL to IQ4_NL 13/8 (p = 0.38); FP8-OpenRouter to 3.25 BPW 9/5 (p = 0.42). PROVISIONAL (search digests only, not read): provider card reports DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 at 82.7 on Terminal-Bench 2.1 with DeepSeek's own harness in minimal mode at max effort; a Terminal-Bench 2.1 aggregator lists the figure as a provider run. The entry's Artificial Analysis 79% snippet was not re-found. All of these differ from the 49.4% stock number mainly by harness, which is the post's point, and none is a primary read.

### Verdict

dive-verified. Cite for: harness lift is model-dependent (DSV4F +20 tasks, 22/2; GLM no detectable change) and costs 2.2-2.5x time/tokens per solve. Do not cite as omp selection evidence (HS-4 decided). The 8bit-KV row is an uncontrolled flag, not a KV-quantization estimate.
worth_investigating only as an input row for the HS-4/HS-5 omp packet; nothing to adopt; external numbers cannot gate.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] harness-selection-and-integration.md: HS-5: attach DSV4F-0731 +20/89 (22 gained / 2 lost) and GLM +4 (9/5 churn), 1.09-2.16x time per solve, as external un-remeasured omp outcome evidence alongside the Pi-vs-OMP counter-claim.

## intake-1355#record — Training Agent Harnesses Like Model Weights (page heading "Harness Training")

- **URL**: https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research, tool_implementation, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1351#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Harness improvement as discrete program search: frozen task LLM (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B FP4 on SGLang), improver agent (GPT-5.5 high via Codex CLI) proposing one bounded harness edit per epoch, greedy promote (git fast-forward) or reject, learning.md memory across epochs.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Blog raw HTML (sha256[:16] a0570d17ea2d9f1f) Training Setup: "Frozen Task LLM: Qwen 3.6 35B A3B FP4 quantized model"; "Agent used as src/trainer/estimator.py:AgenticEstimator for training the harness: GPT-5.5 high via Codex CLI". Repo README (HEAD 0900e8ac) describes greedy fast-forward promotion of src/policy/core.py with candidates under refs/candidates/.
1. Determinism as the precondition for credit assignment: with SGLang --enable-deterministic-inference, seed 12345, <=10 running requests, radix cache off, plus environment scrubbing and replay certification, promotion rate rose from 7.9% (1,223 decisions) to 25.2% (131 decisions); author calls this descriptive, not causal.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Figure caption "Candidate promotion/keep rate 7.9% -> 25.2%". The footnote states pre-determinism April 11-May 25 (1,223 decisions) vs post June 25-July 1 (131 decisions), "a descriptive comparison rather than a controlled causal estimate". SGLang flags are in the footnote: "--enable-deterministic-inference --random-seed 12345 ... --disable-radix-cache ... --max-running-requests 10".
   - anchor `Blog, LLM Determinism section, figure caption + footnote 14` @ https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/ raw HTML sha256 a0570d17ea2d9f1f..., retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The pre-determinism period covers April 11–May 25 (1,223 decisions); the post-determinism period covers June 25–July 1 (131 decisions). Because the periods differ in duration and experiment volume, this is a descriptive comparison rather than a controlled causal estimate."
2. Promoted TB training mechanisms (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B FP4, 38 tasks): reasoning-runaway repair on length cutoff without a tool call (+5, the largest step), force-submit at step >=108 when edits exist (+1), write-arg replay cap (+1), threshold change (+1). Four tool-call validation/repair commits (drop empty junk args, strip extra_forbidden fields, oversized-call steer, partial-call-as-oversized) produced 0 net solves, improving only first-try valid calls from an already ~98% ceiling. Separately from the SWE run: persist-fix prompts, git-state guard, pipefail. Commit hashes in the post are not present in the public repo (rewritten on branch v2-exp-tb-0706).
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] The promoted-commit table (9 rows) is verified verbatim. #2 b5e204fa "Drop empty junk args from tool calls before validation" "19 total: nginx-request-logging (net 0 against the previous baseline)"; #5 a0dccbb4 extra_forbidden strip "17 total (same solved set; first-try valid tool calls improved from 97.78% to 97.95%)"; #6 925f790a oversized-call steer 97.95%->98.29% same set; #7 14ef7381 length cutoff with no tool call -> disable thinking + prompt "22 total (+5)"; #9 ca49ae96 partial-tool-call cutoff treated as oversized, 97.62%->97.69% same set. Tool-call-repair commits net 0 is CONFIRMED. Narrowings: (a) the hashes do NOT resolve in the public repo. Branch v2-exp-tb-0706@82df0c48 carries 9 rewritten "candidate" commits whose diffs match the mechanisms (e.g. 067f7a1 adds the "single, concise tool call" prompt, 55e81f2 LENGTH_CUTOFF_ABORT_THRESHOLD=3, 6f19538 extra_forbidden). (b) The net-0 tool-call repairs were measured on the TRAINING model (Qwen3.6-35B FP4) at ~98% first-try-valid calls, a ceiling. (c) pipefail and the git-state guard are SWE-run observations, not TB table rows.
   - anchor `Blog, "All 9 promoted harness changes" row 7` @ https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/ retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "if llm response had a length cutoff with no tool call inside, disable thinking and steer with prompt"
   - anchor `Blog, promoted-changes row 5 (a0dccbb4)` @ https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/ retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "17 total (same solved set; first-try valid tool calls improved from 97.78% to 97.95%)"
   - anchor `Blog, New capability section, GPT-OSS 20B paragraph` @ https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/ retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "For GPT-OSS 20B, the fatal step rate fell from 9.4% to 1.9%, allowing trajectories to run longer (attempted steps increased from 5.6k to 9.5k) and enabling some runs to convert late. Tool call failures per step remained essentially unchanged at ~31%."
3. A harness trained once on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B lifts TB2.0 (89 tasks, single run per model, temperature 1.0, 38 train tasks inside the eval set) by +2 to +5 tasks per model: GPT-OSS-20B 14->17, GPT-OSS-120B 27->32, DeepSeek 3.2 36->39, MiniMax 2.5 37->39. The large gaps vs Terminus 2 are cross-setup comparisons against others' leaderboard runs. SWE-trained -> TB transfer is reported on a 76-task panel (GPT-5.5, MiMo). GPT-OSS-20B and 120B respond in opposite directions on token cost.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] fig1 (docs/harness_result.png at HEAD 0900e8ac, identical labels to the blog fig1) re-read: MiniMax 2.5 43.8/41.6/42.2; DeepSeek 3.2 43.8/40.4/39.6; GPT-OSS-120B 36.0/30.3/18.7; GPT-OSS-20B 19.1/15.7/3.1 (trained/untrained/Terminus 2). No CSV/JSON of eval results is committed on main or either experiment branch. EVAL RUNS: all seven own-run values are exact integers over 89 (43.8=39, 41.6=37, 40.4=36, 36.0=32, 30.3=27, 19.1=17, 15.7=14), and only the Terminus 2 leaderboard bars carry error bars. The eval config table lists temperature 1.0 and no trial count. So each model/harness pair was evaluated in ONE run, and the GPT-OSS-20B trained-vs-untrained delta is exactly 3 tasks. The training tasks (38) overlap the 89-task eval ("It's obvious that there is overlap").
   - anchor `docs/harness_result.png (blog fig1_vs_terminus2.png), GPT-OSS-20B group` @ workofart/harness-training@0900e8acc124caa8b4c7354cfeeb6750873d395e (transcribed on-image text): "GPT-OSS-20B 19.1 | 15.7 | 3.1 — Terminal-Bench 2.0 accuracy (% of 89 tasks)"
   - anchor `Blog, eval config table, GPT-5.5 column, Terminal Bench Settings` @ https://www.henrypan.com/blog/2026-07-18-harness-training/ retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Same settings; 76-task panel, the remaining 13 the API rejects 4"
4. Author acknowledges overlap: the 38 training tasks are a subset of the 89 evaluation tasks.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Blog Evaluation Results: "Evaluated on Terminal Bench 2.0 all 89 tasks. It's obvious that there is overlap between the training set and the evaluation set. 23 out of 38 tasks were solved by Qwen 3.6 35B A3B FP4".

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (blog raw HTML a0570d17; workofart/harness-training main@0900e8ac + v2-exp-tb-0706@82df0c48 + v2-exp-swe-0706@be571946; docs/harness_result.png). Promoted-mechanism table, the 7.9%->25.2% determinism figures (descriptive, 1,223 vs 131 decisions) and fig1 values are confirmed from raw source. Commit hashes do not resolve publicly (rewritten branch). Eval is established as single-run per model (all own-run bars are integer/89), which sharpens Stage-1's "apparently one run". The 76-vs-89 "inconsistency" is narrowed to GPT-5.5/transfer panels. The tool-call-repair net-0 result is scoped to a ~98%-valid training model. Declined: separate ingestion of the experiment branches (part of this entry's repo; recorded in source_revision here). The predecessor post "What 1,000+ Harness Experiments Taught Me" was not dived; it does not bear on these conclusions.

### Verdict

Adopt the method lessons: determinism before N=1 promotion (descriptive, not causal), a solve/give-back ledger, reasoning-runaway repair. Treat per-model eval gains as single-run, contaminated by train/eval overlap, and 2-5 tasks in size, i.e. noise-sized individually. Do NOT use this post as evidence against malformed-tool-call repair on GPT-OSS-20B: its net-0 repairs were measured on a model that almost never malformed calls. On GPT-OSS-20B repair bought survival and recovery, with ~31% per-step failure persisting and large extra token spend on still-failing tasks. The tension with intake-1351#record's i23 is weak: different model, harness and mechanism, and neither source has a validated ablation.
adopt_patterns: determinism-before-promotion, solve-failure ledger with give-backs, reasoning-runaway repair, invalid-call echo repair are cheap and model-agnostic; numbers are external, single-run and overlap-contaminated.

- updated `handoffs/active/tool-use-eval-contract.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/multi-file-coding-completion-capability.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] autopilot-continuous-optimization.md: Determinism-certification step before harness/prompt candidate promotion (fixed seed, fixed slot count, replay baseline action chain); measure local promotion-signal change first.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] tool-use-eval-contract.md: Solve-failure ledger (ran-out-of-time / failed-verification / no-valid-tool-call, gains AND give-backs), first-try-valid-call %, repair-recovery %, tokens spent on still-failing tasks, observe-only.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified Stage-1 hypothesis, operator-review candidate] harness-selection-and-integration.md: HS-4 mechanism checklist: reasoning-budget exhaustion without tool call, invalid-call echo repair, exit-code masking in test pipelines.

## intake-1356#record — LocAgent: Graph-Guided LLM Agents for Code Localization

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09089
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, search_retrieval, tool_implementation, agent_architecture, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1346#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Parses a Python repo into a directed graph (directory/file/class/function nodes; contain/import/invoke/inherit edges) with entity-ID index, name dictionary, BM25 inverted index and code-chunk index.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] dependency_graph/build_graph.py:13-20 defines node types directory/file/class/function and edge types contains/inherits/invokes/imports. BM25 index via llama_index BM25Retriever (plugins/location_tools/retriever/bm25_retriever.py:105).
1. Agent tools SearchEntity (fold/preview/full), TraverseGraph (type-aware multi-hop BFS rendered as a tree) and RetrieveEntity. In a Qwen2.5-7B(ft) ablation on SWE-bench-Lite, removing SearchEntity (file Acc@5 88.32 to 68.98) or the BM25 index (75.18) costs far more than removing TraverseGraph (86.13; function Acc@10 71.53 to 66.06). The ablated model was fine-tuned with all tools present.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Table 6 values confirmed, but its columns are File Acc@5 / Module Acc@10 / Function Acc@10 on SWE-bench-Lite. The ablation uses Qwen2.5-7B(ft) (§5.6: "Due to budget constraints"), a model fine-tuned on trajectories that used all tools, so removing a tool is confounded with distribution shift. The authors' own reading gives graph traversal and multi-hop a real function-level role: w/o TraverseGraph 66.06 vs 71.53, Hops=1 66.79. Independent: LARGER (2605.16352v1, GPT-5.2 backbone) finds LocAgent at LocBench file Acc@5 65.3, below Claude Code 75.2 and Codex 74.1, and argues graph evidence helps only when delivered inside the lexical search loop.
   - anchor `Table 6 caption and §5.6` @ arXiv:2503.09089v2 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Table 6: The ablation study of our model. The metrics used here are file-level Acc@5, module-level Acc@10, and function-level Acc@10. ... Due to budget constraints, we use the fine-tuned Qwen-2.5-7B as the localization model for these experiments."
   - anchor `evaluation/eval_metric.py:279-378 (cal_metrics_w_dataset), :66-75 (acc_at_k), :381 (evaluate_results)` @ gersteinlab/LocAgent@4935b557326c154bad8e8dcf3747cc8d32d1f387: "assert key in ['found_files', 'found_modules', 'found_entities', 'docs'] | comparison = relevant == total_relevant"
2. Fine-tuned Qwen2.5-Coder-32B approaches the Claude-3.5 agent (SWE-bench-Lite file Acc@5 92.70 vs 94.16) at ~86% lower cost per example. Cost is priced from third-party list prices, not measured. The released 32B checkpoint has open user reports of underperformance.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] Table 5: Claude-3.5 $0.66 / 7 rounds; Qwen2.5-32B(ft) $0.09 / 9 rounds; 7B(ft) $0.05 / 6 rounds. 0.09/0.66 is an 86.4% reduction. The cost footnote says costs are computed from Hyperbolic/artificialanalysis list prices, not measured. The HF "32B-CL" checkpoint was initially a mis-uploaded 7B (repo issue #14, fixed 2025-06-28). Users later reported the 32B underperforming the 7B (#14) and empty outputs under vLLM (#22).
   - anchor `Table 4, LocAgent rows (File Acc@1/3/5, Module Acc@5/10, Function Acc@5/10)` @ arXiv:2503.09089v2 html: "Qwen2.5-7B(ft) | 70.80 | 84.67 | 88.32 | 81.02 | 82.85 | 64.23 | 71.53 | Qwen2.5-32B(ft) | 75.91 | 90.51 | 92.70 | 85.77 | 87.23 | 71.90 | 77.01 | Claude-3.5 | 77.74 | 91.97 | 94.16 | 86.50 | 87.59 | 73.36 | 77.37"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2503.09089v2 (identical in v1): "reaching up to 92.7% accuracy on file-level localization while improving downstream GitHub issue resolution success rates by 12% for multiple attempts (Pass@10)."
3. Loc-Bench V1: 560 Python issues from 165 repos (242 bug, 150 feature, 139 performance, 29 security; categories by GPT-4o). Only bug issues are post-Oct-2024; 194/560 predate it. Patches touching >5 Python files or >10 functions are excluded. GT is function-level edited entities (imports/docs/module-level excluded). An independent audit found 10 invalid instances.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] HF parquet via datasets-server, sha c44cf3b7: 560 rows (242/150/139/29). created_at shows ONLY Bug Report is post-cutoff (2024-10-21..2024-12-17). 83/150 feature, 89/139 performance and 22/29 security instances predate 2024-10-01, back to 2015-11. So 194/560 (34.6%) have no contamination mitigation. Categories were assigned by a GPT-4o classifier (App B). Stage-1 reported_results mislabelled Table 4 file values (Acc@1 as Acc@5) and one Table 7 module value. LOCATED: the abstract 92.7% is Qwen2.5-32B(ft) File Acc@5 on SWE-bench-Lite. CONFIRMED by re-scoring: the shipped Claude-3.5 SWE-bench-Lite outputs reproduce all seven Table 4 cells exactly. NARROWED: contamination mitigation covers only the 242 bug issues (194/560 predate Oct 2024); the downstream 12% is the Claude-3.5 localizer, not 32B(ft) (+7.6%); cost is list-price arithmetic; the ablation uses a model trained with every tool. ### Verdict

Loc-Bench V1 plus eval_metric.py is a usable ZERO-DECODE instrument for scoring DCP discovery. Emit ranked found_files/found_entities per instance and score File Acc@5/@10 and Function Acc@10. Budget for 526 base_commit checkouts (shallow fetch-by-SHA, not 37 GB of full clones, or a stratified subset), strip ".__init__", and treat 10 instances as noisy. Do NOT treat the headline accuracies as achievable locally: they are Claude-3.5 agent numbers, and an independent GPT-5.2 re-run got LocBench file Acc@5 65.3. Do NOT read the ablation as "graph traversal is useless": it is a confounded 7B(ft) ablation; the defensible prior is lexical/BM25 entry first, graph second. Do NOT vendor or redistribute the dataset: it has no licence. Only the bug subset is post-cutoff; report bug-only and all-category scores separately.
worth_investigating as an instrument, not a component: Loc-Bench + its evaluator could score DCP discover_candidates (ColGREP vs GitNexus neighbourhood vs BM25) for file/function Acc@k with zero LLM decode.

### Claims (as verified)

0. Separates find from act using oracle localization: every arm gets the gold-changed files and only the rendering varies. Covers all 70 usable multi-file SWE-bench Verified instances (71 selected, astropy-8707 excluded because its gold patch fails). 32 of the 71 are django.

So nearly half the sample is django.

1. Pre-registered H2 failed: structured tiers (UML class skeleton plus signature+doc for the rest of the gold files) did not beat keep/drop (edited units verbatim, other units replaced by name placeholders). Result 23/70 vs 25/70, 10 discordant pairs, exact McNemar p=0.754. Scope: static digests of the edited files only, single-shot, oracle localization, Claude only, Python. Underpowered for small effects.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] Table 4: tier vs keep/drop is 4 vs 6, p=0.754. experiments/resolve/results/ANALYSIS.md matches. I recomputed exact McNemar from analysis.json resolved_ids and got 4/6 p=0.754, 7/3 p=0.344, 7/1 p=0.070. Three narrowings. (a) The keep/drop arm does not delete the remainder. src/dhcm_ng/context_arms.py:102-103 replaces every dropped unit with a placeholder "# <kind> <name> (omitted)", so the null compares signatures and UML skeletons against a name-level list. It does not compare skeletons against nothing. (b) The tier and skeleton renderings cover only the non-gold units inside the gold files (arms_v2.py:219-228). Skeletons of other repository files were never tested. (c) The paper calls this a "powered null", but only 10 pairs are discordant. The exp-014c README concedes "at ~36% base resolve and N=70, only large effects reach significance".

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (arXiv:2607.09691v1 raw HTML; integrallis/act-context@fd115351d0ab742993aa5d7006f1369fb15b6e74; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1; epyc-root@d372c953 handoff). Every number in Tables 1-4, the H3 follow-up, the probe table and the 6/70 flip rate matches raw HTML and the committed derived JSON. McNemar p-values and tokens per resolve were recomputed independently. Narrowed: (a) keep/drop keeps "(omitted)" name placeholders, so the null is skeleton/signature vs name list, and it covers only the rest of the gold files; (b) "powered null" is 10 discordant pairs; (c) the flip rate is one same-day replicate pair through an unpinned alias; (d) probe cells moved by up to 3 between runs. Reproducibility overturned in part: the single squashed public commit cannot prove pre-registration timing, per-instance harness reports are missing, and the shipped analyze stage yields N=0. OUR-CODE claim OVERTURNED: DCP has no enforced codemap budget band; codemap_only is a greedy downgrade rung, and the live seed budget is 2000 tokens with no bands. Adversarial: no independent critique or reproduction found. The pith.science review page returned empty. CodeCompass (2602.20048) is not a contradiction: single author, 258 trials, 30 tasks, one FastAPI repo, about navigation not edit rendering, and the paper scopes its null the same way. SWEzze (2603.28119, Jia/Barr/Mechtaev) is the deployable, learned counterpart. Its oracle-guided distillation plus a fine-tuned compressor reports 51.8-71.3% token cuts and +5.0-9.2% resolution across three frontier LLMs, which is independent directional support for "compress to edit ingredients". The act-context keep/drop is unit-level gold keep, not SWEzze's genetic/delta-debugging search. Surfaced sources: SWEzze and ContextBench recommended for ingest; CodeCompass declined (see ledger). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: operator selected for Stage-2b: arXiv:2603.28119 "Compressing Code Context for LLM-based Issue Resolution" (SWEz -> intake-1365#record; arXiv:2602.05892 "ContextBench: A Benchmark for Context Retrieval in Coding Agen -> intake-1366#record. Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): arXiv:2602.20048 "CodeCompass: Navigating the Navigation Paradox in Agentic Code.

### Verdict

Hold as a PRIOR, not a gate. Once the edit site is known, rendering the rest of the edited files as signatures/skeletons bought no resolve signal over a name-only list. Summaries of code answered almost no behavioural questions. Do NOT conclude that codemaps of neighbouring files, or codemaps used for navigation, are useless: never tested. Do NOT conclude compressed beats full: p=0.07 and location-hinted. Do NOT treat the repo as a verified pre-registration or a one-command reproduction: harness reports are absent and git history is squashed. Most actionable read-across for DCP: the live packer can downgrade a HIT file (one that holds the likely edit site) to codemap_only under a 2000-token budget. That is signature-only rendering of the edit site itself, the representation this paper and its App D(b) (signature-only 0.06) rate worst. Before changing anything, measure a local flip rate with identical-arm reruns: 6/70 at N=70 dwarfs n=3/arm.
adopt_patterns: instrument design (oracle find/act split, expressibility checks, pre-registration, McNemar with measured noise floor) and the prior "spend editing budget on verbatim edited units, not skeletons or summaries" transfer; the numbers (closed cloud model, oracle ceilings, N=70, marginal p) cannot gate DCP without local re-measurement.

Candidate DCP-6 A/B: codemap_only band ON vs OFF at fixed slice budget on a local coder role.
