## intake-1358#record — Deep Agentic Search for Repository-Level Code Question Answering: An Empirical Study

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01507
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, search_retrieval, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1346#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. On SWE-QA (15 Python repos x 48 questions = 720 per condition, 4 models) a ReAct agent with a pre-built vector index reached 65.2% Pass vs 46.2% for a LangChain Deep Agent (planner delegating exploration to an isolated grep/read sub-agent that returns a condensed result); semantic search also cost less than half per correct answer ($0.32 vs $0.74 pooled).
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] arXiv 2608.01507v1 Sec 5.1 and Table 3: pooled Pass 65.2 vs 46.2, Fail 21.9 vs 34.4. Sec 5.2: $/Pass pooled 0.32 vs 0.74. Recomputed from Table 3 as mean $/q divided by mean Pass: 0.839/2.607=0.322 and 1.372/1.849=0.742. The pooled $/Pass is dominated by Gemini 2.5 Pro cost (0.5675 and 0.6749 $/q). The Deep arm has 2,878 runs (Sec 5.3).
   - anchor `Sec 5.1 (Table 3)` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 (only version; html sha256_12 4df0dc8195e6) retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Semantic reached a Pass rate of 65.2% against 46.2% for Deep, and a Fail rate of 21.9% against 34.4% for Deep (Fig. 5)."
1. Coordination breakdown (planner-to-sub-agent hand-off fails to produce OR integrate a usable result) caused 41.8% (414/990) of Deep failures; about 91% of those still produced a fluent confident answer (consistent with 9.4% no-answer in Table 5). The taxonomy does not separate lossy condensation of the sub-agent return from planner mis-specification or integration failure. Sub-agent summarisation is an unablated harness choice, and in LangChain deepagents the "condensed result" is the sub-agent's own final message, not a separate summarizer.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] Table 5: coordination breakdown is 41.8% (414) of 990 Deep failures (not ~991). The 91% figure is prose only (Sec 6.4) with no count. It matches Table 5, where the pooled no-answer rate for coordination breakdown is 9.4%; Semantic has 0 such failures, so the pooled rate is the Deep rate. The mechanism is defined as a hand-off that "fails to produce or integrate a usable result" (Sec 6.2). It lumps planner mis-specification, sub-agent failure and integration loss together, so condensation loss is NOT separated. Sec 8 lists "how sub-agent results are summarised before they are returned" as an uncontrolled harness choice. Coding was done by Claude Sonnet 4.6, with author-author kappa 0.78 on mechanism (400-failure subsample). Appendix A.2 lists only the default tools (write_todos, task, grep, ls, glob, read_file) and says nothing about how results are returned. deepagents@c08cae69 subagents.py:698-707 returns the sub-agent's own last non-empty AIMessage, with no separate summarizer model. The paper does not pin a version, so this is indicative only.
   - anchor `Sec 6.4 The Deep Agent signature; Table 5` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Coordination breakdown accounted for 41.8% of Deep failures and has no counterpart in a flat retrieval agent, and a further 13.5% were non-terminating loops. [...] in 91% of these cases the agent still returned a fluent, confidently worded answer rather than reporting that anything had gone wrong"
   - anchor `Sec 6.2 Failure taxonomy (mechanism definition)` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "a coordination breakdown, in which the planner to sub-agent hand-off fails to produce or integrate a usable result and which is possible only for the Deep Agent"
   - anchor `Sec 8 Threats to Validity, Construct validity` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "a harness still carries design decisions that are not part of the paradigm it implements, including the wording of its system prompt, how its tool descriptions are phrased, how sub-agent results are summarised before they are returned, and how many steps it permits."
   - anchor `libs/deepagents/deepagents/middleware/subagents.py:698-707` @ github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents@c08cae693e0036fcd45d979a7dfa3a7e306a0515 (HEAD 2026-09-13; the paper does not pin a version): "# Walk back to the last AIMessage with non-empty text."
   - anchor `src/api/routes/chat_delegation_reports.py:146-158 (summarizer prompt, text[:12000], role worker_summarize); chat_delegation_config.py:129-132 (220 tokens), :125-128 (2800 chars), :151-157 (handles on, 2600 chars)` @ epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1: ""implementation details and final recommendation. Max 12 bullets, no fluff.\n\n""
   - anchor `src/api/routes/chat_delegation.py:203-220 (architect turn 0 always breaks, so computation turns where fetch_report could run are unreachable); orchestration/prompts/architect_synthesis.md:13 (advertises fetch_report)` @ epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1: "If the report contains `[REPORT_HANDLE ...]`, you may call `fetch_report(report_id)` to lazily load full content before deciding."
2. The Semantic lead holds for all four models, but pooled magnitude is set by one model. Pooled Pass gap is 19.0 pp with Gemini 3 Flash and 8.7 pp (57.1 vs 48.4) without it. Gemini 3 Flash supplies 65% of the summed per-model gap. Without it, cost per correct answer is still about 1.8x higher for Deep.
   - *narrowed*: [CONFIRMED] Table 4 Pass diffs are 5.6, 10.0, 49.6 (Gemini 3 Flash) and 10.6 pp; all significant after FDR. Recomputed from Table 3 with all four models: Semantic 65.18, Deep 46.23, gap 18.95 pp. Without Gemini 3 Flash: Semantic 57.13, Deep 48.40, gap 8.73 pp. Gemini 3 Flash contributes 49.6 of 75.8 summed pp (65%). Mean score gap is 13.3 with it and 7.5 without. $/Pass without it is 0.40 vs 0.71 (1.8x, vs 2.3x pooled). Gemini 3 Flash Deep made 44.6 tool calls per question vs 8.6 for Semantic.
   - anchor `Sec 5.1 (Table 4)` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "a Pass rate difference in favour of Semantic of 5.6 percentage points for Gemini 2.5 Flash, 10.0 for Gemini 2.5 Pro, 10.6 for Qwen3-235B, and 49.6 for Gemini 3 Flash."
3. Bundle comparison, not a clean delegation test: the Deep arm has no index and different tools; authors state tool space and orchestration change together by design, so "delegation hurts" and "no index hurts" are not separable.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Sec 4.1 says equalising tool sets "would collapse the two conditions into one". Sec 8 (Construction validity) says tool space and orchestration co-vary by design. Sec 9 lists a compute-matched baseline as future work, not done.
   - anchor `Sec 8 Construct validity` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The two paradigms differ in both their tool space and their orchestration, and these two aspects co-vary by design."
4. Authors scope the result to read-only single-question QA over an indexable snapshot; they cannot observe context-hygiene benefits in long multi-task sessions and suggest (untested) a hybrid router: index by default, delegated exploration when needed.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Sec 7 reads the result narrowly (single-question read-only QA over an indexable snapshot) and names long multi-task sessions as open. Sec 7 and Sec 9 propose the index-default plus delegated-exploration router and leave it untested.
   - anchor `Sec 7 Discussion` @ arXiv:2608.01507v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "We therefore read our result narrowly: on single-question, read-only question answering over an indexable repository, the accuracy cost of the coordination outweighs its context-hygiene benefit."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (arXiv:2608.01507v1, html hash unchanged from Stage 1; deepagents@c08cae69; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3). Tables 3/4/5 and the 41.8%/91% figures re-verified. Corrections: the Deep failure denominator is 990. The 91% is prose-only but consistent with Table 5 no-answer 9.4%. Removing Gemini 3 Flash more than halves the pooled gap (19.0 to 8.7 pp). The paper cannot tell condensation loss from planner mis-specification: the mechanism code lumps "produce or integrate", and sub-agent summarisation is an admitted, unablated harness choice. The deepagents default returns the sub-agent's last message, not a separate summarizer, so the paper's "condensed result" is not the same object as our worker_summarize pass. Our-code mapping CONFIRMED in detail but NARROWED: most delegations historically ended in verbatim rescue. The lossy summary only reaches the architect or user on turn-cap and timeout paths. On those paths the raw report is on disk but unreachable to the architect, because the architect decision loop never runs computation turns. Declined dive-surfaced source: DeepRepoQA 2608.24221 (method paper, not a matched hand-off comparison). | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): arXiv:2608.24221 DeepRepoQA: Code Repository Question Answering with Deep Agent .

### Verdict

Do NOT cite this paper as evidence that summarising sub-agent reports causes wrong answers. It measured a bundle (no index + delegation + default LangChain harness), and one cloud model supplies most of the gap. Its hand-off category does not isolate condensation. DO take away three things. Silent confident-wrong answers after a hand-off are a real failure class worth labelling locally. Our architect cannot currently follow its own [REPORT_HANDLE] pointers. On timeout and D|Approved paths the user can receive handle+summary text instead of the full report. These are code facts at 5a9442d3, independent of the paper's numbers.
worth_investigating: cloud models, default LangChain harness, judge-scored, dominated by one model, confounded arms; external numbers cannot gate. The silent confident-wrong hand-off mechanism is well evidenced and maps onto live code, warranting local measurement.

## intake-1359#record — What Does Context Compression Cost an Agent? Interaction Costs Unrevealed by Task-Completion Metrics

- **Verification**: dive-verified

### Claims (as verified)

0. In a synthetic 24-turn planning environment (IRBench, 10 tasks, hidden constraints), compression raised retrieval tool calls in all 6 model x regime cells (5 of 6 significant after Holm) while completion changes were non-significant in every cell at the pre-specified ratio.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Abstract; Sec 3.4; Table 7. Retrieval Holm family p = .002, .002, .002, .004, .023, .088 (Qwen High is the non-significant cell). Completion all p>=0.125 at 5x. IRBench: 10 tasks per instance, 24-turn horizon, 10 seeds, temperature 0.3; models deepseek-v4-flash, qwen3.7-plus, GPT-5.5, all via API.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.16370v1 (only version; html sha256_12 bff7d81d5200) retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Retrieval tool calls increase in every one of our six model–regime comparisons and account for almost all of the added interaction, while execution calls remain approximately unchanged; five of six retrieval increases remain significant after Holm correction."

[...] Serving details are recorded in the reproducibility appendix. [...] per-run tool-call logs were aggregated to condition-level totals before storage."
   - anchor `src/repl_environment/file_exploration.py:68,81 (peek increments the generic counter and logs an event with file_path); src/graph/file_artifacts.py:47-73 (spill writes pointer, no counter); src/repl_environment/state.py:423-432 (events serialized only in REPL checkpoint)` @ epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1: "self._exploration_log.add_event("peek", {"n": n, "file_path": file_path}, result)"
1. Nearly all extra calls are retrieval (re-fetching dropped state); execution calls stay flat ("reacquisition cost").
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Table 4 execute 17.3 -> 13.9 while retrieval 22.2 -> 63.2. Table 7 execute delta is ~0 in 5 of 6 cells. Sec 4.6.4: the GPT-5.5 D-Irrelevant response instead reduced execute calls.
2. At equal 5x budget a deterministic fact-preserving extractive digest kept tool calls near Full (39.0 vs 37.4), while sliding deletion roughly doubled them (71.4). The completion contrast (83% vs 72%) is cross-batch and untested; Sliding-5x completion itself varied 66-72% between batches. The extractive digest is a designed control and says nothing about LLM summarizers.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Table 5 numbers confirmed (Full 80%/37.4, Sliding 72%/71.4, Summary 83%/39.0). The dagger note says Sliding comes from the severity-sweep batch while Full and Summary come from a separate batch. The same Sliding-5x condition reads 66% in the oracle batch (Table 6), and Full ranges 80-86% across batches. No paired test is reported for the 72 vs 83 completion gap. The tool-cost gap (39.0 vs 71.4) is large relative to batch spread (Full tools 37.4-39.5). Limitation 6: the extractive digest is a designed control, not an LLM summarizer.

3. Oracle re-injection of externally queryable state (D*) cut tool calls about 51% under sliding (72.9 -> 35.8); re-injecting history-only state (R*) raised completion +12pp but left tools high. On DeepSeek at a 265-token digest, random atom selection matched a hindsight oracle, and a fabricated-D digest raised retrieval 57%. That content effect vanished at a 100-token digest and was weaker and differently expressed on GPT-5.5.
   - *narrowed*: [PARTIAL] Table 6 reports TOOLS: Sliding 72.9, +D* 35.8 (-51%), +R* 69.0 (-5%, n.s.). The abstract says restoring state removes "roughly half of the retrieval cost". Random vs Hindsight (Table 8) holds at B=265 on DeepSeek only (Limitation 11). D-Irrelevant +57% (20.4 -> 31.9, Table 9) holds at B=265 only; at B=100 it is null (p=0.60, Sec 4.6.3). On GPT-5.5 the irrelevant digest does not exceed the sliding baseline and acts via fewer execute calls (Sec 4.6.4).
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 (Table 6)` @ arXiv:2608.16370v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "Restoring D∗ removes most of the compensatory retrieval (72.9 → 35.8 tools) and recovers most of the completion gap (66% → 80%)."
   - anchor `Sec 6 Limitations item 11` @ arXiv:2608.16370v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "the GPT-5.5 subset replicates the content intervention (Sec. 4.6.4) but does not re-run the selection controls, so we do not claim the selection null is cross-model."
4. Environment-dependent: in ALFWorld (state re-observable) sliding compression caused no retrieval surge; author says magnitudes should not be generalized.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Sec 4.7 / Table 10: ALFWorld 18 tasks x 5 seeds, paired delta retrieval-like = -0.13 (39/35 split), completion 10.0% vs 8.9%. Limitation 1 disclaims transfer of magnitudes.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.16370v1 retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "We do not claim compression has a fixed cost: in a second environment (ALFWorld) the same operator produces no retrieval surge"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE (arXiv:2608.16370v1, hash unchanged; arXiv:2608.01056v1 abstract and body grep; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3; epyc-root@d372c953). Tables 4-10 re-verified. Narrowed: the Summary-vs-Sliding completion contrast mixes batches, the oracle table reports tools, and the selection null plus the +57% content effect are DeepSeek/B=265-bound. Release status: no code/data; a referenced reproducibility appendix is missing. The claimed CompressAgent corroboration measures a different object (static control-context compression). Our-code check: Stage-1 actionable "Phase 4c peek() count" mis-targeted. P4c is closed and belongs to the MCP bash-compressor; the orchestrator spill/peek path has no re-fetch counter, though one is derivable from checkpointed peek events. Declined dive-surfaced source: CompressAgent 2608.01056. | 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: Operator DECLINED (named so they are not re-derived): arXiv:2608.01056 Control Under Compression: Reliability Frontiers for Tool-Using.

### Verdict

Adopt the INSTRUMENT, not the numbers: for any compaction/compression A/B, log per-task re-fetch (retrieval) calls beside completion, because completion can stay flat while re-queries double. Do NOT quote the 83-vs-72 completion contrast as a controlled result, and do not cite CompressAgent as independent replication. Do not assume our spill/peek or CF path already records re-fetches; it does not.
adopt_patterns for the measurement (report retrieval-vs-execution interaction cost beside completion for any compaction A/B); no model/kernel change. Not the numbers: synthetic env, single author, 10 seeds, cloud models, effect vanishes in ALFWorld.

Our production llama-server instances are single-model, and the orchestrator :8000 is not a router, so the built-in provider cannot be used against either. A PR needs a prior maintainer "lgtm". External deps are exact-pinned (.npmrc save-exact=true, min-release-age=2); only internal workspace packages use ^0.85.1. Pi has no permission system (root README.md:41), so containerisation is the documented boundary.

### Verdict

earendil-works/pi and badlogic/pi-mono are ONE repository. oh-my-pi is a detached fork of it, not a sibling. Fix the two handoff passages and the two intake-1204#record sentences; HS-6 capability wording is otherwise unaffected. At source, pi uses the simple call verb everywhere (main loop and compaction), so a models.json samplingParams lever reaches the openai-completions body. That is necessary, not sufficient, and still unverified on the wire. Do NOT conclude the built-in llama.cpp provider works against our single-model llama-server or orchestrator: it is router-only. worth_investigating: HS-4 is operator-owned, HS-1f needs a scored write-up, and one live request is owed (samplingParams x_* keys top-level against orchestrator /v1). Nothing deployed.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (HF bucket joelniklaus/harness-optimization-results via API tree listing + resolve downloads; metadata updatedAt 2026-08-12T10:35:03Z; no revision id exposed; about 100 MB of summaries, configs, traces and logs, no bulk download). Born dive-verified from raw run data. Three findings bear directly on intake-1351#record. (1) The bucket mixes two searches: four iteration dirs, including both 17.1% runs, are Gemma-4-26B-A4B on vLLM, and heldout/ is Gemma-only. So intake-1351#record's bucket bullet overstates the GPT-OSS candidate range and mislabels 3/5-trial screens as single sweeps. (2) Provider rate limiting pervaded iter00-iter16 and both adjudications, then vanished from iter18 on, alongside six harness-commit and three evaluator changes. The one statistically clear gain in the campaign coincides with that regime change. (3) Within each regime, the 28 GPT-OSS candidates are statistically homogeneous, and same-commit re-runs fall 1.5x from screen to adjudication. That is classic winner's-curse plus noise. Named candidates matching all three Sept-10 classes exist, but none separates from its pool. Targeted fix subsets show path-repair 0/14 and malformed-write 1/16. At low reasoning, malformed tool-call rejections were 0 in 1,798 stock calls, while stop-after-unrun-edit was 44% of voluntary stops. No dive-surfaced sources warrant ingestion: patch diffs and candidate commits are unpublished, and huggingface/harbor-hf was already declined under intake-1351#record.

### Verdict

Use this bucket as evidence about MEASUREMENT, not about which patch works. Screens of 39 dev tasks at 3-5 trials on GPT-OSS-20B have about 3-5pp sweep SD. Candidates in the same infra regime were indistinguishable (p = 0.24 and p = 0.50). The only large shift coincides with provider rate limits disappearing. Do NOT cite any Aug per-iteration score, including iter23 path-argument-repair 11.97% or iter29 12.82%, as a patch effect. Do NOT cite 17.1% as a GPT-OSS result (it is Gemma). Do NOT treat heldout/ as GPT-OSS held-out data. DO take two base rates as motivation for local measurement. Stock OpenCode + GPT-OSS-20B-low stopped right after an unexecuted edit in 44% of voluntary stops. Malformed-call rejection was essentially absent (0/1,798) at low reasoning. The small-model stop failure is mostly "hand execution to the user" or "stop after writing", not "announce to self then stop".
adopt_patterns. Nothing to lift as a component. The patterns are measurement-protocol ones: fingerprint every unit (harness commit, evaluator digest, provider route); re-run the seed in each infra regime before comparing candidates; test candidate homogeneity before calling a winner; mark non-comparable runs explicitly; measure failure-mode base rates from traces before building a fix.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (fxtwitter JSON for status 2_091_903_115_985_654_136 + figure HQfuy8EaIAEoKW0.jpg orig + meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B@a4e59da5 + CodingBenchmarkResults summary.json x20). Born dive-verified. All 10 harness values were read by pixel calibration against gridlines, and every one lands on the k/150 grid. The post is a long-form text note, not image-only as intake-1352#record recorded. Arithmetic in the text checks out except a minor openclaw token/output-share inconsistency. The cache-adjusted frontier claim cannot be verified. Harness versions, reasoning setting, and OpenCode stock-vs-patched are not stated anywhere. The model is open weights (Apache-2.0, dense 29.6B) but its arch is absent from v9. A new cross-grid rank comparison gives rho +0.76 vs Gemma-4 and -0.21 vs GLM-5.2. Declined dive-surfaced sources: the Muse-Glimmer model card / HF blog / Meta methodology report (arch not in v9, already declined in intake-1258#record; the TB2.1 51.7 figure is recorded in notes) and Muse Code review blogs (different model).

### Verdict

Take away three things. (1) For a ~30B dense open model on terminal tasks, opencode, qwen-code, mini-swe-agent and pi form a top cluster (37-41%). It sits at least 10.7pp above the rest, including hermes (24.7%), codex, openhands, openclaw and claude-code. Within-cluster gaps of ≤4pp are NOT resolved: 50 tasks x 3 trials. (2) pi and opencode are the cheapest per attempt. Harnesses differ ~2.8x in re-sent input tokens, which is prefill load on our hardware. (3) "Rankings do not transfer" is too strong as a general rule: this order agrees with Gemma-4-26B SWE-bench Pro (rho +0.76) and disagrees with GLM-5.2 (-0.21). Rank looks tied to model class, with benchmark as a secondary factor. Do NOT conclude that opencode wins on our stack. The model cannot run on v9, reasoning and versions are unstated, serving was a remote GPU provider, and external numbers never gate a local decision. Do NOT read the hermes result as disqualifying on its own: on GLM-5.2 hermes beat opencode (39.6 vs 28.0).
adopt_patterns: nothing to adopt as a component. Patterns for the HS-4 bake-off: repeated trials on a frozen held-out split, per-harness input tokens per attempt and prefix-cache hit share as first-class columns, and cost (for us, prefill compute) per solved task next to pass@1.

## intake-1364#record — LARGER: Lexically Anchored Repository Graph Exploration and Retrieval

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16352
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: search_retrieval, agent_architecture, context_management, tool_implementation, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1356#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism: each grep result from a CLI agent is aligned to repository-graph anchors. A deterministic block is then appended to that same grep output, with no LLM producing it: confidence-filtered neighbours (k=10, theta=0.5, so fuzzy-name edges at 0.5 pass), Leiden community labels and execution flows, read from per-file JSON sidecars capped at 20 neighbours. The agent still produces the final localization. The paper reports no retriever-only (no-LLM) LARGER arm.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] §5.1 Implementation (k=10, theta=0.5, no additional tool calls). App E.1-E.4: Table 11 confidence hierarchy; sidecars capped at 20 neighbours; "The block appended to each grep result is generated deterministically from the sidecar index ... no LLM is used to produce it". E.4 prompts show the agent outputs files_to_modify JSON. Tables 1-5 contain no non-agent LARGER row; BM25 is the only no-LLM method.
   - anchor `Appendix E.4, "Graph evidence format"` @ arXiv:2605.16352v1 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The block appended to each grep result is generated deterministically from the sidecar index (Section E.1); no LLM is used to produce it."
   - anchor `§5.1 Implementation` @ arXiv:2605.16352v1 html: "All in-house LLM-based methods use GPT-5.2. LARGER uses default hyperparameters k=10 and θ=0.5; the sidecar graph index is built once per repository and reused across issues, and runtime augmentation occurs inside the agent's existing search loop with no additional tool calls."
   - anchor `src/context_discovery.py:87-119 (discover_candidates; max_files=8 at :92), :13-16 docstring` @ epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d31b716f64d408ed4a3908fc4bc22c69b1: "GitNexus runtime dependency (GitNexus is a dev-time CLI/MCP tool, not importable here)."
1. Re-run numbers, file level on LocBench (Table 1; Acc@K = all GT files in top-K). BM25 23.4/49.3 Acc@1/@5. Agentless 56.1/68.9. Codex 63.2/74.1. LocAgent 56.0/65.3. CoSIL 54.6/68.2. Claude Code (Opus 4.6, not same backbone) 65.1/75.2. LARGER-Fixed 72.9/87.0. LARGER-Tuned 77.1/89.1, where Tuned is a per-repository oracle hyperparameter pick on the test set. All other LLM rows use GPT-5.2. MuLocBench Acc@1/@5: Codex 28.0/50.0, LocAgent 17.9/25.7, Claude Code 29.2/54.9, LARGER-Fixed 27.4/55.7. The paper misdescribes LocBench (Loc-Bench_V1) as 560 issues from "5 repositories"; the dataset has 165. Function-level numbers are reported for baselines only, never for LARGER.

MuLocBench: 51.9 to 57.9 on GPT-4.1, 63.2 to 68.6 on GPT-5.2. Mean runtime also drops, e.g. LocBench GPT-5.2 96 s to 60 s. The prompts differ only in a grep-output interpretation block (App E.4). So appending graph neighbours to lexical hits helps an LLM agent by +5.4 to +8.1 Recall@5 points. This is agentic, cloud-model and Python-only evidence, with no CIs.

No official code or data release was found.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (arXiv:2605.16352v1 raw HTML + TeX source + Figure 1 PNGs; morenomjc/larger-cli@d4e70289; local GitNexus 1.6.8 dist; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3). SETTLED: (1) intake-1356#record's quoted re-run figures are exact (LocAgent 56.0/65.3, BM25 23.4/49.3, Claude Code 65.1/75.2, LARGER-Fixed 72.9/87.0). But Claude Code is Opus 4.6, not GPT-5.2, and LARGER-Tuned is a test-set per-repo oracle. The "5 repositories" misstatement is confirmed in three places (§5.1, Table 10, App C). (2) The "graph helps ONLY when embedded" claim is an inference from cross-system comparison, not an ablation. Table 3 removes components inside LARGER on MuLocBench only, backbone unnamed, runs and variance unstated. The LocAgent re-run collapses at function level (Acc@5 11.7), so it is weak evidence against separate traversal. (3) The fairest evidence is the Figure 1 matched toggle: +5.4 to +8.1 File Recall@5 on 2 backbones × 2 benchmarks. (4) No official code; the only implementation is an unofficial MIT CLI that excludes benchmark reproduction. The method as evaluated needs an LLM agent to rank; only the evidence block is deterministic. NEW internal inconsistencies found: OpenHands Table 1 vs Table 4; cost Table 7 vs Table 9; Recall@1 delta mislabelled as Recall@5; LocBench telemetry 374/560. DESIGN NOTE: the pattern is what GitNexus 1.6.8 augment already does; not cited by the paper. Declined surfaced sources: larger-cli, Codebase-memory (2603.27277), CoSIL (2503.22424).

### Verdict

Take away a PRIOR: attaching cheap, deterministic, confidence-filtered 1-hop graph neighbours to lexical hits helped LLM agents by about 5-8 Recall@5 points and cut steps, on two cloud backbones in Python. For DCP (non-agentic, zero-decode, max_files=8), implement GitNexus expansion as an attachment to ColGREP/lexical hits in the same single pass, not as a separate traversal stage. DCP has no loop to hold a separate stage anyway. Score it against lexical-only at a FIXED candidate budget. Do NOT conclude that expansion dominates lexical in DCP. LARGER's recall-dominance theorem needs a non-evicting context (App A, Pi "does not evict"), and DCP's budget packer evicts, so appended neighbours displace lexical hits. Do NOT cite "graph helps only when embedded" as established; no same-agent separate-tool arm exists. Do NOT use the paper's LocAgent row as evidence against graph traversal: its function-level collapse suggests a broken re-run. Do NOT use its accuracies as local targets: agentic, GPT-5.2, LocBench accuracy denominator unclear (374/560 telemetry). Function-level effect of LARGER is unknown.
worth_investigating as a DESIGN PRIOR, not a component. The pattern (lexical hit plus confidence-filtered 1-hop expansion) is cheap, deterministic, zero-decode and already implementable from GitNexus. The paper only shows it helps an LLM agent. It needs local retrieval-only scoring at a fixed candidate budget via the Loc-Bench instrument from intake-1356#record before it enters DCP defaults.

## intake-1365#record — Compressing Code Context for LLM-based Issue Resolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28119
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: context_management, training_distillation, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, search_retrieval)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1357#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism. OCD is an offline, oracle-guided search. A genetic algorithm first finds a context subset that lets the model produce a passing patch. Hierarchical delta debugging then shrinks it at file, then function, then block level until it is 1-minimal. A context counts as sufficient if at least half of the majority-vote patches pass the tests. The search priority uses gold-patch files, failing-test coverage and gold-patch symbol overlap. Sufficiency is judged by Qwen3-Coder-30B on 10K sampled SWE-smith issues. This yields 3,157 instances with a sufficient context: 41 repos, 156,545 AST segments, 8.4% positive. SWEzze is Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B with a LoRA adapter (r=16, alpha=32). It scores each segment (method/function/class_header/block/file fragment) against a query built from the issue and the fault location. It then fills a token budget greedily and replaces dropped units with "# ... N lines omitted" placeholders. It needs no oracle at inference. The inference code takes issue, fault_location, context and found files, but no gold patch or tests. The paper does not report OCD oracle cost.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] §1 paragraph 5, §3.1-3.4, Algorithm 1, §4 of the v1 HTML. OCD code: artifact/OCD/compress/core/context_searcher_ga.py:51 (GA population 8, 5 generations) and :156-158 (defaults). Inference: artifact/SWEzze/reranker.py:1101-1112. The compress() signature takes issue_description, fault_location, initial_context, found_files, found_edit_locs and must_keep_paths; there is no gold patch or test input. The LoRA defaults in train_reranker.py:87-88 and :1473-1474 (r=16, alpha=32) match §4; the Zenodo README example (r=64, alpha=128) does not. The role bonuses at reranker.py:585 (+0.30) and :603 (-0.15) do not appear in the paper. Oracle-call cost is not found in the source.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction, paragraph 5` @ arXiv:2603.28119v1 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "These distilled examples from the SWESmith dataset (Yang et al., 2025) serve as the training signal for SWEzze, a cross-encoder fine-tuned from Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B (Zhang et al., 2025b) via parameter-efficient LoRA adaptation (Hu et al., 2021)."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction, paragraph 5 (last sentence)` @ arXiv:2603.28119v1 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "At inference time, SWEzze operates without oracle access, scoring segments and assembling compressed context through budget-aware greedy selection."
   - anchor `§3.4 OCD on SWE-Smith` @ arXiv:2603.28119v1 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "From this dataset, we uniformly sampled 10K issues, and distilled Agentless’ contexts for these with OCD by using Qwen3-Coder-30b to evaluate context sufficiency."
   - anchor `§3.1 Search Space Representation (placeholder rendering)` @ arXiv:2603.28119v1 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "When a unit u is excluded, we substitute it with a template τu that indicates the location and magnitude of the omission (e.g., # ... N lines omitted, where N denotes the number of source code lines in the original unit)."
   - anchor `artifact/SWEzze/reranker.py:124-125 and :585 (Zenodo 19248411 artifact.zip md5 b5ec44b5f58624dbf51fe77dd94a2265)` @ zenodo.org/records/19248411 artifact.zip, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The fault location is already provided elsewhere in the patch-generation\n prompt. | score += 0.30"
1. Headline, narrowed. Setup: SWE-bench Verified (500 instances), Agentless top-10 context, downstream models GPT-5.2, DeepSeek-V3.2 and Qwen3-Coder-Next. SWEzze resolves 289/261/210 vs 266/239/200 with no compression. So "+5.0-9.2%" is a RELATIVE gain; in absolute terms it is +2.0 to +4.6 pp. "51.8-71.3%" is the cut in TOTAL repair-step tokens (prompt plus completion); prompt tokens fall 60.3-73.4%. There is one run per cell, with no variance, no significance test and no temperature reported. My unpaired two-proportion z-test gives p about 0.14-0.52 vs no compression (paired McNemar cannot be computed: the artifact has no resolved labels). Giving the fault location with NO context resolves within 0.4-1.6 pp of full context.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Table 2 and §5.2 give absolute +4.4/+2.0/+4.6 pp, which are the relative 9.2/5.0/8.6%. I recomputed the Table 3 reductions (60.3/69.1/73.4 prompt, 55.9/51.8/71.3 total); they match. I searched the v1 text for runs, seeds, temperature and statistics: none reported ("significantly" at §1 is not a test). My unpaired z-tests vs No_compression: DeepSeek z=1.39 p=0.164, Qwen-Next z=0.64 p=0.52, GPT-5.2 z=1.46 p=0.143. patches.jsonl holds only instance_id and model_patch, so paired McNemar is not computable.

3. Release. The Zenodo artifact (CC-BY-4.0) ships the OCD and SWEzze training/inference code plus compressed contexts and patches for each model and arm. It covers only 283-355 of the 500 instances per arm and has no resolved labels. It contains NO fine-tuned checkpoint or LoRA adapter and NO OCD training corpus. The README inference example points at the base Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B, and its argument name does not match the constructor signature. The released reranker also adds hand-tuned role bonuses (for example definition +0.30, generic_utility -0.15) and file caps that the paper does not describe. The base model is Apache-2.0 with 595.8M parameters. Peer-review status: listed in the ASE 2026 preliminary table of contents.

The ASE26 ToC HTML contains the entry "Compressing Code Context for LLM-Based Issue Resolution" by Jia, Barr and Mechtaev.

The authors sampled 30 GPT-5.2 instances that SWEzze failed but full context solved: 5 failed from distracting retained code, 8 from imprecise segment scoring and 17 from disrupted syntactic structure. That contradicts §3.1, which says the hierarchical segmentation keeps candidates well-formed. The authors also concede that minimality is defined relative to the oracle model and its generation parameters. The whole evaluation is Python only.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (arXiv:2603.28119v1 raw HTML, the only version; Zenodo 19248411 artifact.zip md5 b5ec44b5...; msv-lab/SWEzze@abb748d1; ASE 2026 ToC; epyc-root@d372c953; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3). I checked every table number against raw HTML and recomputed the percentages. The bearing dive (intake-1357#record) described SWEzze as "+5.0-9.2% resolution". That range is RELATIVE: absolute gains are +2.0 to +4.6 pp. The 51.8-71.3% is total repair-step tokens, not context. There is one run per cell with no statistics, and unpaired p is 0.14-0.52. The deployed compressor is confirmed non-oracle: the reranker.py compress() signature takes no gold or test inputs. Its localization is Agentless-predicted, not gold, and it compresses only the supporting context around that given fault location. The artifact ships no fine-tuned weights, no OCD corpus and no resolved labels, and covers 283-355 of 500 instances per arm. Its reranker adds hand-tuned role bonuses that the paper omits, so no ablation isolates what the OCD distillation contributes. OUR-CODE check against DCP at orchestrator 5a9442d3: CONFIRMED that the DCP unit is the file, with modes full / slices (ColGREP line ranges, context_discovery.py:171-222) / codemap_only (ast signature skeleton). The ladder is at context_assembly.py:283, the greedy pack at :321, and the live seed budget is 2000 (chat_delegation.py:256, call at :287-290). CONFIRMED that the handoff anchors DCP-2/3/6/7 are at lines 42/43/46/48. Adversarial pass: no independent critique or reproduction found. SWE-Pruner Pro (2607.18213) does not cite SWEzze. Surfaced sources: SWE-Pruner, LongCodeZip, SWE-Pruner Pro and Maniple/fact-selection were all declined (see ledger).

### Verdict

Treat SWEzze as evidence for a RENDERING and a UNIT, not as a component we can deploy. Once an edit site is localized, keeping selected AST segments verbatim and replacing the rest with "N lines omitted" placeholders gave a small, untested gain (+2.0 to +4.6 pp, single run) at about 6x compression. Summaries and token-level compression gave nothing (LLMLingua-2 was flat or negative). Do NOT conclude that SWEzze is available: no weights are released, and the base Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B with its heuristics is not SWEzze. Do NOT conclude that compression improves resolution: the gains fall inside plausible run-to-run noise, and no-context comes within 1.6 pp of full context in this setup. Do NOT carry the numbers into DCP: they come from a single-shot Agentless pipeline with predicted localization, closed and cloud models, Python SWE-bench only. DCP seeds have no fault-location step.
adopt_patterns, not adopt_component. The deployable component is not released: there is no LoRA adapter or fine-tuned checkpoint, and the training corpus is absent. Rebuilding it means running OCD (Docker SWE-smith, a GA/HDD search with 5 votes per candidate, a 30B coder oracle). The transferable patterns are: segment-level keep/drop with "N lines omitted" placeholders, keeping the localized edit site while compressing only supporting context, and oracle-sufficiency labelling as a design for future training data. The external numbers are small, single-run and not statistically tested, so under MEASUREMENT.md they can motivate a DCP-6 arm but cannot gate anything.

- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`

## intake-1366#record — ContextBench: A Benchmark for Context Retrieval in Coding Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05892
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, search_retrieval, context_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1357#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Construction. 1,136 tasks, 66 repo names (67 distinct repo_url) and 8 languages, drawn from SWE-bench Verified, Multi-SWE-bench, SWE-PolyBench PB500 and SWE-bench Pro. The pipeline is: dedup (4,497 to 3,981 to 3,100), difficulty selection (1,500), then manual review. Gold contexts are line spans {file, start_line, end_line, content} traced from the gold patch through call, inheritance and data-flow dependencies. The annotators were six authors plus experts with 3+ years of experience. A second annotator did a compactness pass, and sufficiency was checked by having GPT-5 generate 5 patches from the context alone (at least one must pass F2P+P2P). The paper reports no inter-annotator agreement statistic. There are no contamination controls: all tasks come from public benchmarks, and Appendix K suspects Devstral 2 contamination. Table 1 totals reproduce exactly from data/full.parquet (4,548 files; 522,115 lines; per-language counts).
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] §2.1-2.3, Table 1, App F-G of v3 raw HTML. Parquet re-derivation: 1,136 rows, 0 duplicate ids, 66 repo / 67 repo_url, languages python 512/js 153/ts 119/go 104/c 68/rust 63/cpp 60/java 57, gold files 4,548 and lines 522,115 exact, per-language file and line counts exact. No kappa or agreement number anywhere (searched §2.3, App F). §2 says validation passes by LLM sufficiency OR "high inter-annotation consistency", so a failed sufficiency check does not remove a task. selected_500 status column: fail 179/500, meaning undocumented. created_at is present only for 174 Lite rows: 2013-01-25 to 2023-07-29.
   - anchor `§2.3 Step 3 Expert Annotation, first paragraph` @ arXiv:2602.05892v3 html, retrieved 2026-09-14 UTC: "The annotation is conducted collaboratively by six authors of this paper and a group of engaged expert developers over four months."
   - anchor `§2.3 Context Verification` @ arXiv:2602.05892v3 html: "We then consider an annotated context sufficient if at least one generated patch successfully passes the official test suite, including both fail-to-pass and pass-to-pass test cases."
1. Scaffold headline, narrowed. Table 2 is a single GPT-5 run per scaffold on the 500-task Lite set. mini-SWE-agent has the best file precision/F1 (0.709/0.634) and block precision/F1 (0.369/0.375). Agentless has the best line precision/F1 (0.318/0.376 vs 0.301/0.312). SWE-agent, OpenHands and Prometheus have higher file recall (0.726/0.733/0.717 vs 0.682) and higher Pass@1 (0.490/0.490/0.512 vs 0.472). No variance or significance is reported. The scored context is the context the agent DECLARES in a prompted <PATCH_CONTEXT> block before submitting, so the table partly measures compliance with that format prompt. "Scaffolds do not consistently beat the bash baseline" holds. "Barely beat" holds only for context metrics, not Pass@1.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Table 2 cells copied from raw HTML. The comparison against the paper framing (RQ1 answer "does not necessarily improve") is computed from those cells. §2.5 and App C show scored context is the agent-declared <PATCH_CONTEXT> (context_aware.yaml template). SWE-agent config uses temperature 1.0 and the mini-SWE-agent sample config uses 0.0. No repeated runs are reported. Narrower than the abstract "yields only marginal gains": the gains are not uniformly marginal (Agentless line F1 +0.064; Prometheus Pass@1 +0.040) and are mixed in sign.

2. Model and dynamics findings (mini-SWE-agent, Lite). Table 3: block F1 0.375-0.422 and line F1 0.311-0.344 across GPT-5, Sonnet 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Pro and Devstral 2. GPT-5 has the highest block/line recall but lower F1 and Pass@1 than Sonnet 4.5. Table 5 explored-vs-used "Usage Drop" is 0.179 (GPT-5), 0.196 (Sonnet), 0.431 (Gemini) and 0.435 (Devstral); Drop = 1 - |final ∩ gold| / |explored ∩ gold| (App H Eq. 10). The recall-to-Pass@1 correlation is asserted from 4 models with no statistic.
   - *unaffected*: [CONFIRMED] Tables 3, 4, 5 and App H Eq. 8-10 from raw HTML. Leaderboard JSON matches. The "positive correlation between context recall and downstream task success" (§3.2) has no coefficient; with 4 points Gemini has the lowest recall and Pass@1, but GPT-5 has the highest block/line recall and the second-lowest... actually mid Pass@1 (0.472 < Sonnet 0.530), so it is not monotone.
   - anchor `Table 5 (Efficiency / Redundancy / Usage Drop)` @ arXiv:2602.05892v3 html: "GPT-5 | 0.591 | 0.487 | 0.179 | Claude Sonnet 4.5 | 0.658 | 0.708 | 0.196 | Gemini 2.5 Pro | 0.529 | 0.558 | 0.431 | Devstral 2 | 0.616 | 0.672 | 0.435"
3. Zero-decode scoring is possible. contextbench.evaluate scores any JSONL row {instance_id, traj_data: {pred_files, pred_spans: {file: [{start, end}]}, pred_steps?}} with no LLM call. It needs one git checkout per (repo, base_commit): 1,136 distinct. Each is a blob:none clone plus a detached worktree under CONTEXTBENCH_TMP_ROOT (default: system tmp). Summed GitHub repo size for the 67 URLs is about 10.8 GB (upper bound on history; worktree footprint is not stated anywhere). Data is 26 MB (full.parquet). The repo LICENSE (Apache-2.0) covers the shipped data/ parquet; the HF card has no licence field. The released scorer does NOT reproduce the paper metric definitions. It micro-averages and computes no F1. It drops errored or empty-prediction instances from the aggregate. Empty gold gives coverage 1.0 and empty prediction gives precision 1.0. EditLoc falls back to the gold patch and sets recall == precision. It routes .h to the C parser.

Footprint: gh api size summed over 67 repo_url slugs = 10.84 GB (Lite-bearing repos 8.49 GB). HF tree: full.parquet 26,102,607 bytes, ungated, no licence tag.
   - anchor `contextbench/parsers/custom_parser.py:30-31; contextbench/evaluate.py:535,541` @ EuniAI/ContextBench@1436c28a8eb95496da4ea69ad458b9f8a8eb7d61: "- pred_files: List[str] - final context file list | - pred_spans: Dict[str, List[dict]] - {file_path: [{"start": int, "end": int}, ...]} | valid = [r for r in results if "error" not in r] | # Micro-average for final metrics"

Edge cases: gold=0→cov=1.0, pred=0→prec=1.0.""" | # Fallback: use gold patch if present (parquet provides it as `patch`)"
4. Gold-data defects (independently verified subset of a third-party audit, EuniAI/ContextBench issues #2-#7, 2026-08-12, all unanswered). 3 tasks have empty gold. 1,509 distinct (task, path) gold entries are /workspace-absolute. 42 Multi-SWE-bench tasks carry bare solver scratch files in gold (reproduce.cpp, repro.mjs, reproduce.sh), which suggests trajectory-seeded gold for those rows. gold.py:23 lstrip("./") mangles dot-paths. 45% of C++ gold files are .h. The auditor also reports 88 tasks (7.8%) with at least one gold path unresolvable at base_commit (I confirmed the mechanism, not the count). RQ5 robustness (Jaccard 0.9518) covers 82 tasks at an unstated granularity. Appendix A counts (4.4k/3.9k/3.1k/1.2k final) disagree with the main text (1,136).

C++ gold distinct files: .h 94 of 209 = 45.0% (matches #4). gold.py:23 lstrip bug confirmed in code; I found about 14 tasks with dot-leading paths vs #2 "10 tasks", different heuristic. The 88-task / 0.9798 ceiling needs per-commit tree fetches I did not run: mechanism confirmed, count NOT re-verified. Appendix A "1.2k" vs 1,136 confirmed at App A/B text.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (arXiv:2602.05892v3 raw HTML; EuniAI/ContextBench@1436c28a8eb95496da4ea69ad458b9f8a8eb7d61; HF Contextbench/ContextBench@c2855792; data/full.parquet sha256 prefix 2f56535bdc73; epyc-orchestrator@5a9442d3; epyc-root@d372c953). The surfacing description (1,136 tasks, 66 repos, 8 languages, human-annotated gold, v3) is CONFIRMED, and Table 1 re-derives exactly from the parquet. NARROWED: (1) the "Bitter Lesson" scaffold claim is one GPT-5 run per scaffold on Lite. mini-SWE-agent wins file/block precision and F1, but Agentless wins line F1, and three scaffolds beat it on file recall and Pass@1. It also scores agent-declared context, not observed reads. (2) "Human-verified" gold includes contexts that failed the GPT-5 sufficiency check (the pipeline reconciles them by consensus; selected_500 status shows fail 179/500, undocumented). 42 Multi tasks carry solver scratch files, and no agreement statistic is reported. (3) The released scorer implements different aggregation from the paper (micro, no F1, errored instances excluded, vacuous 1.0 edge cases, EditLoc on gold patch), so paper numbers are not reproducible from the release and no per-instance outputs ship. ADVERSARIAL: an unanswered third-party audit (issues #2-#7) was verified at mechanism level, with the empty-gold, /workspace, scratch-file and C++ .h counts re-derived. No published critique or reproduction was found. OUR CODE: DCP task-root discovery whitelists no C/C++/Go/Rust/Java extensions (code_search.py:164), so a C++ arm would score zero until that is fixed. Surfaced sources (Prometheus, the upstream benchmarks, the SWE-ContextBench name-collider, the GitHub audit) are all declined; see ledger.

### Verdict

ContextBench is the better PRIMARY instrument for DCP discovery. It scores exactly what DCP emits: a set of file plus line-range spans, including the non-edited dependency context a pre-assembled bundle must carry. It measures precision, which is the budget question, and not only edit-site hit rate. It is Apache-2.0 via the GitHub repo, 26 MB of data, and covers Python (512) plus C/C++ (128). Score it zero-decode by emitting pred_files/pred_spans per instance. Do NOT trust contextbench.evaluate aggregates as shipped: wrap them to macro-average per task, count errored or empty predictions as zero recall, drop the 3 empty-gold tasks and the 42 scratch-file tasks, fix lstrip, and ignore EditLoc. Report File and Line granularity; do not report Symbol for C/C++. Do NOT read the paper tables as "simple beats scaffolds": that is one closed-model run on declared contexts. Do NOT treat its C++ as representative of ggml/llama.cpp-style code: the C++ repos are header-only libraries (json, fmt, simdjson, Catch2). Gold median is 254 lines per task, so under the live 2000-token DCP seed budget recall is budget-capped; report recall at fixed token budget.
adopt_component as a local instrument (dataset plus a patched or wrapped scorer), not as evidence. Its external numbers are single closed-model agent runs and cannot gate DCP. The released scorer has verified defects that must be wrapped before any local score is claimed.

## intake-1367#record — Workflow Closure Is Not Scientific Closure in Auto-Research Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26200
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-883#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Position paper: completing a research-like loop internally (idea -> experiment -> writing -> self-evaluation) is "workflow closure" and does not by itself confer scientific standing; trustworthy auto-research should aim for autonomous EXECUTION under NON-autonomous EPISTEMIC control (abstract, v1).
   - *unaffected*: Examined and cleared. The dive verified the claim's own load-bearing phrase verbatim at Section 6 / Abstract - 'autonomous execution under non-autonomous epistemic control' - and confirmed the position-paper framing (self-described position paper, Section 1; cs.SE, no venue in the comments field). The workflow-closure framing was not disputed by any finding in the dive; the corrections below touch the survey/audit claim and the remedies claim only. Row added by the Stage-2 index clerk during re-keying, from the dive's own anchor verification, not from a separate verdict.
   - anchor `Section 6 meta-principle / Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "autonomous execution under non-autonomous epistemic control"
1. Diagnoses a three-level collapse pattern across a survey of >100 papers/repos and a structured audit of 21 representative systems: OBJECTIVE collapse (single-proxy targets replace multi-objective aims), VALIDATION collapse (internal self-evaluation replaces independent validation), ACCEPTANCE collapse (benchmark scores or publication-shaped artifacts replace domain-level critique, reuse, integration) (abstract, v1).
   - *narrowed*: Split the claim. The "21 systems" figure is VERIFIED (21 rows in both Table 1 and Table 2, codes agreeing row for row) and the audit is rubric-based with an inspectable per-system trace. The ">100 papers and repositories" figure is the authors' ASSERTION with no enumeration, search protocol or inclusion log; the 123-entry reference list is consistent with it but is not a survey count. Cite the 21 freely; cite the 100+ only as an assertion.
   - *narrowed*: The 81.0% / 71.4% / 90.5% rates are arithmetically exact over the pool but are explicitly NOT prevalence estimates (§8) and come from a single coder reading public artifacts (B.1, B.4). They may be cited as "the audit coded 17 of its 21 systems L1-strong"; they may NOT be cited as "81% of auto-research systems exhibit objective collapse".
   - *narrowed*: Three refinements the abstract omits, all of which change how the rubric applies to us. (a) A BENCHMARK IS AN L2 FAILURE, not an L2 remedy — benchmark-mediated signals sit inside the producer's inductive boundary (§3.2), which is why ADAS and HGM are coded L2-strong. (b) The operational test is GATING, not presence (§3.4; B.2 Weak-vs-Mitigated). (c) Independence is not multiplicity — cross-family or ensemble review is L2-weak at best (§9; ARIS row). Our planner/critic split across CLIs must never be cited as independent validation.
   - *reattributed*: STAGE-2b 2026-09-14 (intake-1371#record, arXiv 2603.15916): the X. Li (2026) citation is REATTRIBUTED. 1367 cites Li as evidence that nominal architecture search collapses into hyperparameter tuning; the paper asks that question and answers NO - its 94%/6% ANOVA split is an unbalanced one-way ANOVA, a balanced re-analysis gives roughly 50/50, and the variance is driven by the frozen backbone choice. 1367 also misnames the system as Karpathy's autoresearch template; it is the author's own Orze system. The 'agents mostly tune hyperparameters' finding 1367 appears to mean is MLGym (arXiv 2502.14499), cited by Li. The audit codes and rates of this claim do not rest on Li (counterfactual failed: only related-work sentences at paper.txt:338, :404, :492 would change), so no depends_on edge. Source: /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/stage2b/intake-1371#record-REPORT.md.
   - *unaffected*: STAGE-2b 2026-09-14: none of intake-1373#record (Zhang et al., 2605.19156), intake-1374#record (Rios-Garcia et al., 2604.18805) or intake-1377#record (REFLECT, 2605.19196) is load-bearing for this claim. Counterfactual test applied per paper (intake-1377#record-REPORT.md section 4): 1373 is cited at App. A.5 :494 and section 7 :338 as 'compatible', 1374 at App. A.5 :490 as operating 'at a different level', 1377 once in a survey list at App. A.4.3 :486 - retracting any of them leaves the audit codes, rates and taxonomy unchanged. depends_on stays without them; the 2026-09-14 Stage-2 note that ingesting rows 7-9 would create a claim_index 1 edge is withdrawn. If EPYC needs 'manuscript-only review fails to validate' to gate a decision, cite intake-1373#01 and intake-1377#01 directly.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Based on a survey of more than 100 recent papers and repositories in this rapidly emerging area, together with a structured audit of 21 representative systems, we diagnose a recurring and structurally connected failure pattern"
   - anchor `Section 4, audit-pool selection paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "From this corpus, we identify a full-coding audit pool of 21 systems with sufficient loop structure and public information for L1/L2/L3 coding"
   - anchor `Section 4, audit results paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Among the audited systems, L1-strong is 81.0% (17/21), L2-strong is 71.4% (15/21), and L3-strong is 90.5% (19/21)."
   - anchor `Section 8 Limitations` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "The audit covers selected systems with sufficient loop structure and public information for full L1/L2/L3 coding, rather than the full population, so the reported rates should not be read as prevalence estimates across all uses of LLMs in science."
   - anchor `Appendix B.1, coding basis` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Coding draws only on public artifacts, such as papers, repositories, documentation, prompts, and reported workflows."
   - anchor `Appendix B.3, per-system audit trace` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "No system in the audit pool receives a mitigated label on any dimension, under the rules in Appendix B.2."
   - anchor `Section 3.1 L1: Objective Collapse` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "L1—objective collapse occurs when progress under competing, non-reducible scientific objectives is reduced to a single internal signal. The relevant failure is not that a metric is used somewhere in the loop, but that retention, rejection, or ranking is governed by that metric alone."
   - anchor `Section 3.2 L2: Validation Collapse` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "L2—validation collapse occurs when independent validation is replaced by internal evaluation, such as self-review, same-family evaluators, sibling-model checking, or benchmark-mediated signals that remain within the loop’s own evaluative horizon."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 L3: Acceptance Collapse` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "L3—acceptance collapse occurs when domain-level evaluation and integration are replaced by terminal artifacts, such as a score, a report, or a publication-shaped object that the loop produces without any mechanism for the research community to contest, reuse, or integrate it."
   - anchor `Section 3.4, why the collapses are linked` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Adding a second metric is insufficient if validation stays internal. Adding an external checker is insufficient if its output does not gate the loop. Adding a stronger benchmark is insufficient if outputs still have no pathway into external critique or reuse."
   - anchor `Appendix B.2, coding scheme (Mitigated label)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Mitigated: the dimension is addressed by a genuinely external operation that lies outside the system’s inductive boundary and is integrated as a binding condition of loop validity."
   - anchor `Appendix B.2, Weak vs Mitigated distinction` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "Weak captures systems whose external-looking checks are simulated from within the system’s inductive boundary, whereas Mitigated requires a genuinely external operation integrated into the loop as a binding condition of validity."
   - anchor `Section 9, third objection (independence)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "independence is not a property of multiplicity alone: models trained on overlapping data, selected by the same benchmark culture, or rewarded under the same scoring rules can still share the producer’s blind spots."
   - anchor `Section 9, third objection (test for a validator)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "The relevant question is whether the validator introduces an external constraint that the producing loop cannot freely simulate, optimize against, or reinterpret as success."
2. Claims the collapses are correctable design choices, not inherent limits of autonomy, and sketches remedies across objective signal, validation, and output pathway (abstract; Sections 5-6 titles seen, content NOT read at Stage 1).
   - *narrowed*: PARTIAL as evidence. "Correctable design choices" rests on an existence argument from four out-of-pool contrast cases (Lean-4 VML, Latent-Y, Robin, CRISPR-GPT) plus the executable-evaluation family (FunSearch, AlphaEvolve, ERA, Coscientist). The six §6 remedies carry ZERO implementation and ZERO evaluation anywhere in the paper, and the paper concedes no audited system attempts coordinated remediation across all three axes (§5). Cite the remedies as a design checklist, never as validated interventions.
   - *narrowed*: STAGE-2b 2026-09-14 (intake-1372#record, arXiv 2603.24647, now v5): the phrase 'reliability dominates exploration breadth' is 1367's paraphrase, not the paper's - 'reliability' occurs 0 times in v5; the source says 'OOM avoidance matters more than search diversity'. The finding is a correlation between OOM rate and outcome across 9 method means at n=3 seeds, with no significance test (the text says 'significant' but runs none), and KA-14HP had the lowest OOM rate and the worst result. The authors' later n=8 update (repo blog) calls the headline 'thin statistical ice'; Centaur-Opus 4.6 vs TPE p=0.055. 'Classical beats LLM in a fixed space' is therefore model-dependent and must not be cited as settled.
   - *narrowed*: STAGE-2b 2026-09-14 (intake-1370#record, ERA, arXiv 2509.06503 / Nature 2026): 1367 names ERA among systems whose retention is 'gated by external execution rather than by internal evaluators alone' and uses it in the executable-evaluation leg of the correctable-design-choices existence argument. The dive found ERA's external leaderboards are POST-HOC comparison done by the authors (GIFT-Eval against a frozen 2025-05-18 snapshot, deliberately not submitted live; scRNA-seq holdout reported afterwards); in-loop retention is argmax executable TaskScore on the authors' own splits, and there is no human acceptance gate (humans only curate ideas and check code). Under 1367's own B.2 rule ERA is an executable-in-loop exemplar, NOT an external-in-loop (Mitigated) exemplar; the leg holds only in that narrowed form. depends_on edge added.
   - anchor `Section 1, final paragraph before contributions` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "However, these collapses are not inherent limits of autonomy but correctable design choices, and we propose potential remedies for each."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R1 (objective ledger)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "auto-research systems should maintain an objective ledger: a structured record of the aims a candidate output is supposed to satisfy, the evidence for each, the trade-offs among them, and the decision rule used when the loop proceeds despite unresolved conflict."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R2 (Pareto frontier)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "evaluation should operate over a Pareto frontier of non-reducible objectives, so that trade-offs remain visible to the loop and retention decisions preserve them rather than prematurely reduce them to a single notion of progress."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R3 (validator provenance record)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "systems should maintain a validator provenance record: who or what validated each claim, the validator’s independence relation to the producer, the evidence inspected, and whether the result changed subsequent search."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R4 (in-loop validity condition)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "a validator’s signal should enter the research loop as a condition of validity, not annotate outputs after the loop has already decided what counts as progress."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R5 (claim package)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "systems should emit a claim package rather than only a paper-shaped artifact: a set of atomic claims, their evidence traces, code and data provenance, failure cases, known limitations and sensitivities, and a route by which external users can reproduce, contest, or extend the claims."
   - anchor `Section 6, remedy R6 (stopping rules)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "that package should also include stopping rules: conditions under which the system must defer, request external review, or decline to treat an output as ready for domain uptake."
   - anchor `Section 5, opening paragraph (why partial remedies fail)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "First, they are not systematized: added checks are usually ad hoc additions rather than pre-defined discriminative protocols. Second, they remain post-hoc rather than in-loop: external signals rarely become correction signals inside the system’s primary research loop."
   - anchor `Section 6, closing of the L3 remedy` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "In this sense, acceptance is a process, not a score."
   - anchor `Section 9, second objection (benchmarks do not scale)` @ arXiv:2605.26200v1: "The problem arises when these instruments are treated as substitutes for the external operations they can only approximate."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2 DIVE, CLERK CORRECTION TO THE DIVE ITSELF: the dive agent read the SHARED clone at d372c953, not the lane worktree, so its 'ENTRY DOES NOT EXIST' finding is a stale-clone artifact and is NOT recorded here. intake-1367#record and intake-1368#record both exist on origin/main (5406c223..1d9d31e5), as do the 2026-09-14 wiki sections the dive reported missing. Consequence for the anchors and corrections below: the dive's claim_index values 1/2/3 were keyed to a three-claim RECONSTRUCTION from the abstract and have been re-keyed by the clerk against the entry's real 0-based key_claims - dive claim 1 (survey scope) and dive claim 2 (the three collapses) BOTH map to claim_index 1, which carries the audit scope and the collapse taxonomy in one claim; dive claim 3 maps to claim_index 2; and the meta-principle anchor ('autonomous execution under non-autonomous epistemic control') maps to claim_index 0. Every quote_sha256 below is the first 16 hex of SHA-256 over the quote bytes as rendered in the v1 HTML, not a full digest.
- SOURCE REVISION CONFIRMED v1 (submitted 2026-05-25 17:16:52 UTC); no v2+ exists as of 2026-09-14, so the pre-fetched HTML (sha256 3f6ba3bace12edbf…) is current. Comments field: 26 pages, 1 figure, 2 tables. NO VENUE — self-described position paper (§1). License is the arXiv perpetual non-exclusive licence, NOT CC BY, so verbatim reuse beyond quotation is limited.
- AUDIT IS RUBRIC-BASED, NOT NARRATIVE — three-label scheme (Strong/Weak/Mitigated) in Appendix B.2, per-dimension coding rules in §4, and a per-system trace in Table 2 with objective-signal / validator / output-pathway columns. Table 1 and Table 2 were diffed and agree row for row on all 21 systems and all 63 codes; the 81.0% / 71.4% / 90.5% rates were re-derived from the rows and are exact. BUT: single coder, no second coder, no inter-rater reliability, public artifacts only (B.1), and §8 states the rates are NOT prevalence estimates. Any EPYC text quoting those three percentages without that caveat misuses the source.
- CORPUS SIZE IS ASSERTED, NOT ENUMERATED. ">100 papers and repositories" has no search string, date-cutoff table, inclusion log or PRISMA count anywhere in the paper; Appendix A.1 lists only exclusions. Independent check: the reference list has exactly 123 numbered entries, which is consistent with the claim but includes skill/memory substrate and diagnostic-position literature, not only auto-research loops.
- THE FRAMEWORK IS PARTLY DEFINITIONAL. Mitigated requires a genuinely external operation integrated as a binding condition of loop validity (B.2), so "systems whose loop closes internally fail to achieve scientific closure" is close to true by construction, and the audit can find no Mitigated label in its own pool. The empirical content is therefore the ASYMMETRY (L3 least remediated at 90.5%, L2 most remediated at 28.6% weak), not the existence of collapse. The paper's factual load is carried by OTHER groups' measurements — Zhang et al. (117 papers, artifact-aware review), X. Li (10,000 experiments), Ríos-García et al. (>25,000 runs) — none of which are in our index (see dive_surfaced_sources).
- NO INDEPENDENT CRITIQUE OR REPLICATION EXISTS. Three bounded searches returned only the paper itself plus three auto-generated restatement aggregators (bytez.com, awesomepapers.io, pith.science) — RESTATEMENT, not corroboration. No citing works found; the paper is ~3.5 months old. Authors are all at the Yale School of Public Health (biostatistics / public health), not an SE or ML-systems group; cs.SE, not peer reviewed.
- DIVE BRIEF PREMISES CORRECTED — three targets named in the Stage-2 steering do not exist in the repo. (1) There are no 2026-09-14 sections in wiki/autonomous-research.md or wiki/benchmark-methodology.md; the newest heading in both is "Compiled Update — 2026-09-08" and git log for both files ends at ae2427ac (2026-09-08). (2) No "planner/critic formation-payoff deadlock" text exists anywhere in the repo (grep for "formation.payoff" returns zero); the nearest real records are stopped_mid_formation (autonomous-research.md:2314) and the payoff-threshold rows at :477-495. (3) The four benchmark-methodology themes DO exist but are dated 2026-08-16 (ETR-2 computed-guard-never-consulted, :834), 2026-09-07 (absent-vs-measured-zero, :4560-4573) and 2026-09-08 (contention/dropped-arm family, :4843-4849); "stop-time counterfactual" is not a phrase in the repo — the mechanism is the n=5/n=10 sign flip at rebuild :1762-1766 plus the pre-registered two-look sign test at :135-139. Also: the autopilot item is AP-50/task_failed under its own IDs; "OP-20" resolves via master-handoff-index.md:52, not an OP-20 row in the autopilot handoff. Cite the real locators, not the brief's.
- PARTLY RETRACTED BY THE CLERK 2026-09-14 - items (1) and (2) of this note are stale-clone artifacts. (1) is WRONG: the 2026-09-14 wiki sections DO exist in the lane. wiki/autonomous-research.md:5 carries 'Last compiled: 2026-09-14' naming 'the planner/critic formation-payoff deadlock: 8 batches and 24 first-pass critic calls reached zero authoring because payoff evidence was required at formation - a gate whose evidence cannot be produced at the stage it gates is a latch', with the section 'A critic that demands proof of PAYOFF before authoring can livelock a loop without failing anything (2026-09-14, noninf sweep)' at :45, a further 'Compiled Update - 2026-09-14: payoff uncertainty belongs in the experiment, not its formation gate' at :2605, and a back-reference at :70; wiki/benchmark-methodology.md:5 likewise reads 'Last compiled: 2026-09-14' with 2026-09-14 compiled updates at :8, :81 and :112. git log for both files ends at 25f95aa9 (operator wrap-up 2026-09-14), not ae2427ac. Items (3) and (4) STAND as written - the dated benchmark-methodology themes and the AP-50 / task_failed / master-handoff-index.md:52 identification were verified against content that predates the lane. Original dive text follows: STRONGEST APPLICABLE RESULT, recorded so it is not lost: the paper's L2/L3 gating criterion ("Adding an external checker is insufficient if its output does not gate the loop", §3.4) and our own computed-guard-never-consulted family ("a check that produces a verdict without verifying it had anything valid to verify", wiki/benchmark-methodology.md:4848-4849) are the SAME finding reached independently from opposite directions. ### Verdict

worth_investigating: a design-principle paper cannot be adopted as a component; its value is as an audit rubric for our own two loops, which is a dive-then-decide question. Credibility 1: arXiv v1 preprint no venue (+0), <12 months (+1), no known lab in the metadata read (0), no independent corroboration yet (0).
