## intake-1369#record — gflow (runqd) - lightweight single-node Slurm-like job scheduler for shared GPU workstations, with HTTP API and stdio MCP server

- **URL**: https://github.com/AndPuQing/gflow
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: tool_implementation, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-883#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Single-machine Linux scheduler bringing a Slurm-like workflow (queue, GPUs, time limits, AND/OR dependencies, arrays, priorities, hold/release, redo, retries) to a shared workstation without cluster setup; gflowd daemon launches each job itself as a detached bash process group (or tmux session) (README; src/multicall/gflowd/executor.rs).
   - anchor `README.md:16` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "`gflow` is a lightweight scheduler for a single Linux machine. It brings a Slurm-like workflow to shared GPU workstations and lab servers without cluster setup."
   - anchor `README.md:23` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "Submit commands or scripts with GPUs, time limits, dependencies, arrays, and priorities."
1. GPU scheduling is NVIDIA-only: GPUs are discovered and monitored through NVML and handed to jobs via CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES; a GPU carrying any non-gflow NVML compute process is treated as unavailable with an unmanaged(pid=...) reason unless an operator adds that pid to an ignore list (docs gpu-management.md; scheduler_runtime/gpu.rs).
   - anchor `docs/src/user-guide/gpu-management.md:3` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "gflow detects NVIDIA GPUs (via NVML) and allocates them to jobs by setting `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`."
   - anchor `docs/src/user-guide/gpu-management.md:38` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "If NVML reports running compute processes on a GPU, gflow treats it as unavailable (often shown as `Unmanaged`) and will not allocate it."
2. The per-job resource model is exactly gpus, gpu_sharing_mode, gpu_memory_limit_mb, time_limit and memory_limit_mb; host memory is admission ACCOUNTING (detected total minus the sum of declared memory_limit_mb of running jobs). There is no CPU-core, CPU-set, NUMA, socket or memory-bandwidth resource anywhere in src/ or docs/ (src/core/job/model.rs; scheduling.rs).
   - *narrowed*: Self-narrowed at write time so the claim cannot be over-read: memory_limit_mb is admission accounting only. The "no enforcement" and "no CPU/NUMA field" halves are grep NOT-FOUND results over src/ and docs/ at the pinned sha (patterns numa|cpuset|taskset|affinity|cgroup|bandwidth|--cpus|rlimit|MemoryMax), not a quoted positive statement; the anchors cover only the positive resource list and the accounting line.
   - anchor `src/core/job/model.rs:99-106` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "// Resource requirements (hot - checked during scheduling) pub gpus: u32, #[serde(default)] pub gpu_sharing_mode: GpuSharingMode, #[serde(default)] pub gpu_memory_limit_mb: Option<u64>, pub time_limit: Option<Duration>, pub memory_limit_mb: Option<u64>,"
   - anchor `src/core/scheduler/scheduling.rs:58` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "self.available_memory_mb = self.total_memory_mb.saturating_sub(memory_used);"
3. A queued job carries a typed waiting reason - JobHeldUser, WaitingForStartTime, WaitingForDependency, WaitingForResources, WaitingForGpu, WaitingForMemory, WaitingForQuota - plus terminal reasons CancelledByUser, DependencyFailed(id), SystemError(msg); the CLI display collapses Gpu/Memory/Resources into one Resources label (src/core/job/state.rs).
   - anchor `src/core/job/state.rs:74-85` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "pub enum JobStateReason { JobHeldUser, WaitingForStartTime, WaitingForDependency, WaitingForResources, WaitingForGpu, WaitingForMemory, WaitingForQuota, CancelledByUser, DependencyFailed(u32), SystemError(CompactString), }"
4. Scheduling pops a ready heap ordered by (priority, time-limit bonus, FIFO), optionally re-sorted by a Slurm-style fair-share factor with 7-day half-life WITHIN a priority band, then admits greedily: a job that does not fit is skipped with a reason and later jobs that fit start - i.e. unconditional backfill with no reservation for the blocked head job except explicit GPU reservations (src/core/scheduler/scheduling.rs; scheduler.rs).
   - *narrowed*: The starvation consequence (an exclusive head job can wait forever behind fitting smaller jobs) is derived from reading the admit loop, not observed in a run; the positive claim (skip-with-reason, later jobs proceed) is anchored.
   - anchor `src/core/scheduler/scheduling.rs:172-174` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "This only reorders jobs within a // priority band — hard constraints (group concurrency limits, // reservations, memory/GPU availability) are still enforced per job below."
   - anchor `src/core/scheduler/scheduling.rs:311-315` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "if !has_enough_gpus { self.set_job_reason(job_id, Some(JobStateReason::WaitingForGpu)); self.enqueue_if_ready(job_id); continue; }"
5. Quotas per user/project/default: max_running_jobs and max_running_gpus gate starting (job waits with WaitingForQuota); max_queued_jobs is enforced at submission and REFUSES with a human-readable reason string; runtime overrides via gctl quota persist across restarts (src/config.rs; src/core/scheduler/quotas.rs).
   - anchor `src/config.rs:134` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "/// Maximum number of pending (queued/held) jobs; enforced at submission."
   - anchor `src/core/scheduler/quotas.rs:162` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: ""quota exceeded for user '{user}': {pending}/{max_queued} queued jobs""
6. Reservations are GPU-only (a count or specific GPU indices, for a user, over a start time and duration) (src/core/reservation.rs).
   - anchor `src/core/reservation.rs:53-54` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "/// GPU specification (count or specific indices) pub gpu_spec: GpuSpec,"
7. The daemon schedules on events (submit/update/complete/timeout/GPU or memory availability change) coalesced by a 100 ms debounce tick, exposes an SSE /events stream, but still polls NVML on a configured interval, time limits every 10 s and tmux zombies every 30 s (scheduler_runtime/event_loop.rs).
   - anchor `src/multicall/gflowd/scheduler_runtime/event_loop.rs:129` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "let mut debounce = tokio::time::interval(Duration::from_millis(100));"
   - anchor `src/multicall/gflowd/scheduler_runtime/event_loop.rs:31` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "// GPU monitor - polls NVML using the configured interval"
8. HTTP API (axum; /jobs, /jobs/batch, /jobs/{id}/{finish,fail,cancel,hold,release,log}, /events, /info, /status, /health, /gpus, /gpu-processes, /groups/{id}/max-concurrency, /reservations, /quotas, /stats, /metrics, /debug/*) binds localhost by default with NO authentication or RBAC; the submitter identity is whatever USER/USERNAME (or the MCP submitted_by field) says (server.rs; docs multi-user.md).
   - anchor `docs/src/user-guide/multi-user.md:8` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "`gflowd` exposes an HTTP API and does not currently provide authentication or RBAC."
   - anchor `docs/src/user-guide/multi-user.md:5` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "`gbatch` records the submitting username from the current shell environment (`USER` or `USERNAME`)."
9. gflow mcp serve is a stdio MCP proxy to the daemon with exactly 17 tools: get_info, get_health, list_jobs, get_job, get_job_log, get_stats, get_queue_pressure, list_reservations, cancel_job, hold_job, release_job, preview_submit_jobs, submit_jobs, preview_update_job, update_job, triage_job, redo_job. preview_submit_jobs never contacts the daemon - it only validates the MCP-side request shape and expands param sweeps (it cannot predict admission, dependency cycles or quota refusal); triage_job returns the job record, a cleaned log tail (default 80 lines / 20 kB) and hard-coded retry-hint strings keyed on state/reason and log substrings, and states that gflow does not record the process exit code. Mutation safety is a description-text convention ("MUTATES ... require explicit user confirmation"), not an enforced gate (src/multicall/mcp/server.rs, submit.rs, triage.rs).
   - *unaffected*: The maintainer's "17 tools incl. preview_submit_jobs and triage_job" (orx issue #316) was CONFIRMED by counting #[tool( attributes in src/multicall/mcp/server.rs (17). What the two tools DO is narrower than their names suggest and is recorded in the claim itself, not as a correction to the maintainer text.
   - anchor `src/multicall/mcp/server/submit.rs:142` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: ""dry run only validates MCP-side request shape; daemon-side dependency, cycle, and project policy checks still run at submission time""
   - anchor `src/multicall/mcp/server/triage.rs:60` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: ""gflow currently records terminal state but not the process exit code""
   - anchor `src/multicall/mcp/server.rs:435` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "description = "Summarize why a job is queued or failed, including recent log evidence and retry hints. Read-only.""
   - anchor `src/multicall/mcp/server.rs:332` @ github.com/AndPuQing/gflow@598fa6e0257aa675639d629b37e7a39454aa3ad9: "description = "MUTATES scheduler state: submit one or more jobs to the local gflow daemon using a simplified schema. Prefer preview_submit_jobs first and require explicit user confirmation.""

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b (s2b-gflow): the premise that gflow could serve as the lane arbiter / OP-41 admission broker is OVERTURNED for this host on source evidence - no CPU, NUMA or bandwidth resource; NVML-only GPU discovery; greedy backfill; unauthenticated identity. It remains a pattern source.
- Dive-883 surfaced-source row phrase "multi-GPU/multi-socket box" is not supported by the code (no socket or core topology model).
- Brief premise corrections: AP-1526 is not a real handoff identifier; cpu_region_lock.py is a 50 ms polling non-blocking flock with no fair queue.
- 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 2] slurm.schedmd.com/mc_support.html (Slurm multi-core support) - OP-41 checklist value only, no bandwidth model, no ingest recommended; [row 4] orx issue #316 - provenance only, not index-worthy on its own (already in notes).

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, not adopt_component: (1) no CPU/NUMA/physical-core/bandwidth resource, so it cannot express an exclusive region or the sibling-expanded physical-core reservation that the 2026-09-08 region-lock blind spot requires; (2) NVML-only, so our MI210 is invisible; (3) unconditional backfill starves exclusive jobs; (4) no auth and client-asserted identity make quotas and fair-share advisory on a host shared by many agent sessions; (5) OP-41 (operator, 2026-09-08) forbids a scheduler or admission daemon before champion finalised -> production promotion -> reboot, and assigns the design to the operator. Not not_applicable: its waiting-reason ticket, foreign-occupancy-with-reason, submit-time refusal string, event-driven wake, persisted runtime halt override and read/MUTATES MCP split are concrete design inputs for OP-41 and for the lane-arbiter precondition, framed as inputs, not decisions. credibility_score null: a repository with no empirical claims.

- updated `autopilot-continuous-optimization.md`

## intake-1370#record — An AI system to help scientists write expert-level empirical software

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06503
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. ERA mechanism: an LLM rewrites a candidate program, the program is executed and scored in a sandbox, and a flat PUCT tree search (rank-normalised scores, flat prior 1/|T|, c_puct=1 tuned on 16 Kaggle Playground competitions) picks ANY node in the tree to expand, so search can backtrack to any historical node; the returned solution is argmax of the task score; score saturates after 300-1000 nodes; all headline experiments used Gemini 2.5 Flash (Methods, Algorithm 1, arXiv v3).
   - anchor `Methods, Code mutation system` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "This makes our method closer to Flat UCB 48 than any MCTS variant like AlphaZero."
   - anchor `Methods, Code mutation system` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "This global, flat-tree structure allows the system to seamlessly backtrack and branch from any historical node if the current optimization path plateaus."
   - anchor `Methods, Code mutation system` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "score saturates after 300-1000 nodes"
   - anchor `Methods, Code mutation system` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "All experiments in this paper were carried out with Gemini 2.5 Flash, with the improvement with Gemini 2.5 Pro modest."
1. The quality metric is the domain benchmark's own executable score, and external anchoring is split from selection: scRNA-seq selects on a separate CELLxGENE dataset and reports the OpenProblems v2.0.0 overall score (mean of 78 control-scaled metric x dataset values, NaN=0) on the holdout datasets; COVID-19 selects on a rolling 6-week validation window and reports Weighted Interval Score against the CovidHub ensemble; GIFT-Eval uses official val/test splits and a frozen 2025-05-18 leaderboard snapshot, deliberately not submitted live; Kaggle is scored by direct submission to the public leaderboard (Results, Methods).
   - anchor `Results, scRNA-seq` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "To avoid overfitting to the benchmark, we used a separate dataset for ERA optimization (Methods, Supplementary Fig. S1)."
   - anchor `Methods, OpenProblems evaluation` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "Overall score was computed as the arithmetic mean over all 78 measurements (13 metrics computed for each of 6 datasets) with NaN values replaced by 0"
   - anchor `Results, COVID-19` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "Submissions are evaluated using the Weighted Interval Score (WIS), which rewards both accuracy and well-calibrated uncertainty."
   - anchor `Methods, GIFT-Eval` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "we deliberately chose not to submit to the live public leaderboard, restricting our comparison to the May 18th snapshot to ensure a valid, stable baseline."
   - anchor `Results, Kaggle Playground Benchmark` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "and we score code by submitting directly to Kaggle."
2. scRNA-seq results: tree-search implementations beat the corresponding published method for 8 of 9 methods; BBKNN (TS) is 14% above the best published method (ComBat); 40 of 87 ERA methods (6/11 base, 29/55 recombination, 4/9 Deep Research, 1/12 AI co-scientist) outperform every method on the OpenProblems leaderboard (Results, Fig. 2).
   - anchor `Results, scRNA-seq` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "In pairwise comparisons, ERA outperformed the corresponding published result for eight of the nine methods in overall score (Fig. 2b)."
   - anchor `Results, scRNA-seq` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "yielding a 14% overall improvement over the best published method (ComBat 33)"
   - anchor `Results, scRNA-seq` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "(40 of 87) outperform all methods currently published on the OpenProblems leaderboard (Fig. 2d)."
3. COVID-19 results: the retrospective model reaches average WIS 26 vs the CovidHub ensemble's 29 over the 2024-25 season, using data as of 2025-05-01 for the whole season (so data revisions are ignored); 14 strategies beat the ensemble on a 3-week (3 reference dates x 4 horizons x 52 jurisdictions) holdout (Results, Fig. 3, Methods).
   - anchor `Results, COVID-19` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "Overall, our model achieved the highest performance with an average WIS of 26, outperforming the official CovidHub Ensemble’s average WIS of 29."
   - anchor `Methods, COVID-19 dataset` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "thus ignoring potential differences in data available at the date of forecast."
   - anchor `Figure 3e caption` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "14 strategies (10 recombination; two Deep Research; one AI co-scientist and one replicated baseline) outperform the official CovidHub-ensemble for the 3-week (3 reference dates × 4 time horizons × 52 jurisdictions) evaluation period."
4. ERA vs best-of-N: ERA beats best-of-N for every model and both tasks except GPT-5 on batch integration, measured on the VALIDATION set and reported as the best of 3 experiments; Methods says N=128 search nodes while the Table 1 caption says best-of-N=1000 (internal inconsistency, not resolved in the text) (Methods, Table 1).
   - *narrowed*: Recorded as narrowed at birth: the ERA>BoN result is validation-set, best-of-3, and the N is stated inconsistently (N=128 in Methods, best-of-N=1000 in the Table 1 caption). Cite as "ERA beat best-of-N on validation in 9/10 cells", never as a test-set or fixed-N result.
   - anchor `Methods, ERA versus best-of-N` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "We use N=128 ERA search nodes"
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "Performance is measured on the validation set for each task. Bold entries indicate better performance. BoN, best-of-N=1000."
   - anchor `Methods, ERA versus best-of-N` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "We present here the best performance over 3 different experiments (with both Best-of-N and ERA)."
   - anchor `Methods, ERA versus best-of-N` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "outperforms Best-of-N for all models and problems, with the exception of GPT-5’s performance on batch integration."
5. Humans are in the pipeline but not in the search or retention loop: authors manually filtered Deep Research / AI co-scientist proposals, experts manually inspected code for adherence, and datasets were manually selected; the authors themselves distinguish optimising empirical predictive models from genuine scientific discovery (Methods, Results, Discussion).
   - anchor `Methods, Adding research ideas` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "After manually filtering proposals and removing one proposed scRNA-seq batch integration method"
   - anchor `Results, scRNA-seq` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "expert manual inspection of the code solutions proposed by ERA confirmed that nearly all implementations adhered to the requested algorithms"
   - anchor `Discussion` @ arXiv:2509.06503v3: "We would like to emphasize the critical distinction between optimizing empirical predictive models and performing genuine scientific discovery"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b: intake-1367#record's description of ERA ("retention is gated by external execution rather than by internal evaluators alone") is PARTIAL against the primary source. Execution is external to the LLM, but the external LEADERBOARD is a post-hoc report: the tree returns argmax TaskScore on author-built splits (Algorithm 1), and the comparison to OpenProblems / CovidHub / GIFT-Eval is performed afterwards. ERA therefore illustrates executable in-loop validation plus post-hoc external comparison, not external in-loop gating.
- HUMAN GATING: none in the search loop; humans filter proposed ideas, select datasets and inspect code adherence. There is no deployment or promotion step anywhere in the paper.
- 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 7] Google AI Co-Scientist (arXiv 2502.18864) - no recommendation, already operator-declined 2026-09-14 as Stage-2 row 18; [row 8] AIDE (arXiv 2502.13138) - low value per this dive, valid-step-ratio use is carried by intake-1375#record without an entry; [row 9] OpenProblems v2.0.0 batch-integration and GIFT-Eval (2410.10393, id unverified) - not recommended.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns: one protocol pattern transfers (AK-ACC-1: promotion numbers from launches disjoint from keep-deciding pairs, against a frozen baseline); the acceptance architecture does not. Credibility 3: Nature peer review +2, <=12 months since journal publication +1, major lab +1, commercial bias -1, no independent corroboration 0.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- created `autokernel-cross-workload-keep-gate.md`
- updated `autokernel-unified-surface-program.md`

## intake-1371#record — Auto Researching, not hyperparameter tuning: Convergence Analysis of 10,000 LLM-Guided ML Experiments

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15916
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. System/scale: the Orze orchestrator ran two LLM agents (Claude Opus, 819 cycles; Gemini 2.5 Pro, 864 cycles) on a shared filesystem for 27 days on 16 H100s (3,227 GPU-h, ~31% utilization); each cycle an agent writes 3-5 base YAML configs, an automated HP-sweep module expands them to ~25 candidates, and a scheduler deduplicates by config fingerprint; 42,042 candidates were generated and 10,469 executed (25.0%), of which only 1,337 have fully parseable configuration records (Section 2, Section 3.6, Appendix B, G).
   - *unaffected*: 42,042 / 10,469 / 25.0% / 1,337 and the agent-cycle design verified verbatim (Section 2).
   - *narrowed*: CORRECTION (intake-1384#record, Stage-2b round 2): "a scheduler deduplicates by config fingerprint" holds only as EXACT dedup. The Jaccard > 0.9 near-duplicate fingerprint (paper Appendix B, recorded in techniques) is NOT in any public Orze revision, branch or PyPI wheel (git log -S over all refs of warlockee/orze and orze-ai/orze; wheel 4.4.3 grep). Public ingest dedup is an exact canonical-config hash against completed ideas. The 42,042 -> 10,469 reduction cannot be attributed to similarity dedup from public code.
   - anchor `Section 2, Multi-agent coordination` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "Over 27 days of continuous operation, the system generated 42,042 total candidate configurations (including auto-sweep expansions), of which 10,469 were executed to completion (25.0% execution rate; the remainder were deduplicated or deprioritized)."
   - anchor `Section 2, Multi-agent coordination` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "Of the completed experiments, 1,337 have fully parseable configuration records suitable for the ablation and convergence analyses in Section 5"
   - anchor `Section 2, Multi-agent coordination` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "Each agent generates 3–5 base configurations per cycle; an automated hyperparameter sweep module then expands promising bases into variants"
1. The 94%/6% split is a one-way ANOVA on validation AP with groups = backbone x encoder combinations having >=10 experiments, post-bugfix n=838, 12 groups, F=1324.3, eta^2=0.94; the "6% hyperparameter" share is the within-group residual (all non-architecture knobs plus training noise), not a modelled HP factor. Balanced to n=10 per group eta^2 falls to 0.50; within VJepa2 the encoder explains 3.5%, so the effect is overwhelmingly backbone choice (Section 5.4, Table 3).
   - *narrowed*: The abstract's "architectural choices explain 94% ... hyperparameter variation within a fixed architecture explains only 6%" overstates the model: one-way ANOVA over unbalanced groups where DINOv3-B (best 0.556) sits next to VJepa2 (~0.9); balanced eta^2=0.50; encoder effect within VJepa2 3.5%. Cite as "frozen-backbone choice dominated AP variance", not "architecture search beats HP tuning".
   - anchor `Section 5.4, ANOVA method paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "we compute the within-group variance (hyperparameter variation within a fixed architecture) and the between-group variance (across architectural combinations)."
   - anchor `Section 5.4, balanced-subsample sentence` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "confirms a substantial architecture effect even after removing the sample-size imbalance."
   - anchor `Section 5.4, VJepa2-only sentence` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "indicating that the dominant architectural decision is the backbone, not the encoder."
2. Post-bugfix = the 3,003 experiments completed after the fourth infrastructure fix (27 Feb: data leakage, focal loss not wired, attention pooling not wired, eval padding mismatch). A nonlinear least-squares fit of AP*(N)=a-bN^-c (a<=1) on that cumulative-best curve gives c=0.11, R^2=0.93, but random permutations of the same data give mean R^2 0.81 with 0.93 at the 95th percentile; pool-resampling random and TPE converge faster (c 0.99, 1.12); the only from-scratch baseline is 172 uniform samples (AP@50 0.9648 vs LLM 0.9852) (Section 5.2, Table 2).
   - *narrowed*: The power-law fit is not distinguishable from a permutation null at better than the 95th percentile; the "LLM discovers qualitatively better regions" argument rests on n=172 random samples and on AP values (0.985-0.994) inconsistent with the stated champion AP 0.9245.
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Post-bugfix convergence` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "we restrict the primary convergence analysis to the 3,003 experiments completed after the final bug fix (February 27 onward)."
   - anchor `Section 5.2, R-squared permutation caveat` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "exceeds the 95th percentile (0.93) but is not dramatically higher."
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Interpreting the baseline comparison` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "because the pool already contains high-quality configurations—this does not mean random search is superior."
3. The paper frames "do agents default to hyperparameter tuning within a narrow region?" as its question and answers NO (architecture search) - the opposite of how intake-1367#record cites it. Search did concentrate (VJepa2 = 52.6% of all and 100% of the top-100 experiments; only 2.4% of the 108,000 discrete cells explored), and re-exploration was forced by hard-coded prompt diversity budgets (Abstract, Section 5.3, Section 7, Appendix A, F).
   - *reattributed*: Recorded so intake-1367#record's citation cannot be re-derived: the narrowing-to-HP-tuning mechanism 1367 attributes to Li is the question Li answers in the negative. The concentration Li does show (VJepa2 dominance) is leaderboard exploitation with forced prompt quotas, not an objective-collapse measurement.
   - anchor `Abstract, first sentence` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "When LLM agents autonomously design ML experiments, do they perform genuine architecture search—or do they default to hyperparameter tuning within a narrow region of the design space?"
   - anchor `Appendix A, VJepa2 adoption` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "By campaign end, VJepa2 constituted 100% of the top 100 results and 52.6% of all experiments."
   - anchor `Section 5.3, Entropy decay` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "These exploration-exploitation cycles arise from the interplay between exploitation pressure (leaderboard feedback favoring VJepa2) and explicit diversity budgets in the agent prompts that periodically force re-exploration."
4. Infrastructure bugs dominated the campaign: data leakage caused 8,730 experiments to be evaluated on a contaminated split and three silent misconfigurations affected ~8,975 experiments; the author concludes LLM agents "operate on the specification layer but cannot verify the code execution layer" (Appendix A, Section 7).
   - *unaffected*: Bug timeline and the spec-vs-execution-layer limitation verified (Appendix A, Section 7).
   - anchor `Appendix A, Bug timeline item (1)` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "causing 8,730 experiments to be evaluated on a contaminated test set"
   - anchor `Section 7, Configuration verification gap` @ arXiv:2603.15916v1 (arxiv.org/html/2603.15916v1, raw sha256 d970eaa342ae...): "LLM agents operate on the specification layer but cannot verify the code execution layer."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (arXiv:2603.15916v1). (1) intake-1367#record cites this paper for the OPPOSITE of its conclusion and says it used the Karpathy autoresearch template; it used Orze. (2) 94%/6% is a one-way unbalanced ANOVA whose balanced version is 50%; the 6% is residual, not HP-attributed. (3) Power-law R^2 is at the permutation p95. (4) Unreconciled AP scales (0.9245 champion vs 0.985-0.994 in Table 2) and a subset n (2,039) larger than the parseable base (1,337). (5) 8,730 of 10,469 experiments were evaluated on a contaminated split before the first fix, so "10,000 experiments" is not 10,000 valid measurements. 2026-09-15 STAGE-2b ROUND-2 CORRECTION (intake-1384#record): (6) the "Jaccard > 0.9 near-duplicate fingerprint" dedup attributed to Orze appears only in the paper; it is NOT in any public Orze revision, branch or PyPI wheel - public dedup is exact-hash only, so the DA-3 fold row below supplies an exact execution identity (intake-1384#02), not a similarity threshold. (7) Repo moved: github.com/warlockee/orze is archived (2026-04-24, v4.1.1); development continues at github.com/orze-ai/orze (same root commit 984cc98), recorded as intake-1384#record. (8) 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 11] MLGym (arXiv 2502.14499) - citation-correction source only, correction already applied to intake-1367#record; [row 12] Harris & Slivkins (arXiv 2502.00225) - none stated, changes no drafted task.

### Verdict

Do not cite this paper as evidence that an optimization objective collapses LLM search into hyperparameter tuning - it argues the opposite, and its numbers are internally inconsistent (two AP scales, subset larger than base, balanced eta^2 half the headline). Its usable content for EPYC is qualitative: mechanical fingerprint dedup at dispatch beats trusting agents to avoid repeats, and agents cannot verify that a configured knob is actually wired.
adopt_patterns (narrowly: fingerprint dedup at dispatch). Credibility 0: arXiv v1 no venue (+0), <=12 months (+1), no known lab (0), commercial/self-promotion bias - single author evaluating their own product Orze (-1), no independent corroboration (0).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `autokernel-rebuild-program.md`

## intake-1372#record — Can LLMs Beat Classical Hyperparameter Optimization Algorithms? A Study on autoresearch

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24647
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Benchmark: 9 HPO methods (classical TPE, CMA-ES, SMAC, Random; LLM-based LLAMBO-Optuna, LLAMBO-Paper, Karpathy Agent 14 HPs, Karpathy Agent Code; hybrid Centaur) tune nanochat (~50M params, val_bpb) under a 24 h training-time budget per run, 5-min trials on one H200 capped to 80 GB, 3 seeds; fixed space = 14 HPs auto-extracted from train.py by AST (13 numeric + WINDOW_PATTERN categorical), manually ranged; OOM scored as val_bpb=100; LLM inference time EXCLUDED from the budget; main LLM is self-hosted Qwen3.5 0.8B/27B, thinking off, T=0.7 (Sections 1, 3, 5).
   - *unaffected*: Setup verified verbatim (Sections 1, 3, 5; Tables 1-2); budget exclusion of LLM inference time confirmed.
   - anchor `Section 1 Introduction, benchmark paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "we benchmark 9 HPO methods, spanning 4 classical, 4 LLM-based, and 1 hybrid, on Karpathy’s autoresearch task. All methods operate under the same 24-hour GPU training budget with 3 seeds."
   - anchor `Section 3, AST extraction paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "This yielded 14 HPs (13 continuous/integer and one categorical)"
   - anchor `Section 5 opening` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "We exclude LLM inference overhead from wall-time to isolate optimization quality from inference cost."
1. In the fixed space at n=3, classical methods beat pure-LLM methods: mean best val_bpb Centaur-27B 0.9763, TPE 0.9768, SMAC 0.9778, CMA-ES 0.9785, Karpathy Agent (Code)-27B 0.9814; LLAMBO-Paper 0.9862, Random 0.9873, LLAMBO-Optuna 0.9882, KA (14 HPs)-27B 0.9904. The text calls the gap "significant" but reports no statistical test; std across 3 seeds only (Section 5.1, Table 3).
   - *narrowed*: n=3 with no significance test. Centaur-0.8B 0.9766+-0.0008 vs TPE 0.9768+-0.0019 is inside seed noise, so "a 0.8B LLM already suffices to outperform all classical methods" (abstract) is a point-estimate ordering, not a result. At n=8 (authors' blog) TPE moves to 0.9755 and the fixed-space pure-LLM conclusion becomes model-dependent.
   - anchor `Section 5.1, first paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "The top methods by mean best val_bpb are Centaur (0.9763), TPE (0.9768), SMAC (0.9778), CMA-ES (0.9785), and Karpathy Agent (Code) (0.9814)."
2. OOM avoidance tracked final quality better than diversity: top methods have OOM <=16% (SMAC, 36%, is the stated boundary), the bottom four exceed 36%; LLAMBO-Paper is among the most diverse (spread 0.252) yet 48% OOM. This is a correlation across 9 method means, and low OOM is not sufficient: KA (14 HPs)-27B had 1% OOM and the lowest diversity (spread 0.035) and finished last among fixed-space 27B methods (Section 5.1, Table 3).
   - *narrowed*: Correlational across 9 method-level means. Cite as "OOM rate separated top from bottom methods in this benchmark", never as a causal law, and never with the word "reliability" as a quote (0 occurrences in v5).
   - anchor `Section 5.1, second paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "Moreover, OOM avoidance matters more than search diversity: Table 3 shows that the top methods all have OOM rates at or below 16%, while the bottom four exceed 36%."
   - anchor `Section 5.1, second paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "Karpathy Agent (14 HPs) has the lowest diversity by all metrics, converging to a narrow region early."
3. The "LLMs struggle to track optimization state across trials" claim is explicitly scoped to the 0.8B/27B models tested and is INFERRED from OOM rates - LLAMBO variants saw full trial history yet had 48%/61% OOM vs 56% for random - not from a direct state-tracking measurement (Section 1, Section 5.1).
   - *narrowed*: Scoped to 0.8B/27B and inferred from OOM rates of LLAMBO (an LLM-as-surrogate method), not measured on the agent arms. Counter-signal in the same table: KA (14 HPs)-27B saw full history and held 1% OOM. Frontier-model n=8 data (blog) shows Opus 4.6/4.8 LLM-suggested Centaur trials failing at 1.8%/0.2%.
   - anchor `Section 5.1, second paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "LLAMBO (Paper) and LLAMBO (Optuna) observe full trial history yet produce OOM rates (48% and 61%) comparable to random search (56%), suggesting they fail to learn which regions of the search space cause memory failures."
   - anchor `Section 1, Centaur hypothesis paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "struggles to track optimization state across trials reliably, e.g., LLM methods show OOM rates comparable to random search despite observing full trial history."
4. Source-code-editing arm: each trial the LLM receives the current-best train.py plus history and returns a complete replacement file (may change anything except prepare.py imports). It is the only pure-LLM arm near classical methods and needs scale: 0.8B 0.9910, 27B 0.9814, Gemini 3.1 Pro 0.9826, Opus 4.6 0.9770+-0.0027 vs TPE 0.9768+-0.0019; its failure rate falls with model capability (19%/12%/3%/5%); Gemini 3.1 Flash-Lite code edits failed in 87-94% of trials (Section 5.2, A.3, A.11).
   - *unaffected*: Code-arm prompt and numbers verified (A.11, Section 5.2, Table 3); the n=8 tie with TPE is recorded in claim 7, not as a correction of the n=3 figures.
   - anchor `Appendix A.11, Karpathy Agent (Code)` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "The LLM receives the current best train.py source code together with problem context and returns a full replacement for train.py."
   - anchor `Section 5.2, final paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "Scaling the LLM from 0.8B to 27B is essential for unconstrained code editing but provides no advantage for fixed-HP optimization"
5. Centaur mechanism as published: CMA-ES (Optuna CmaEsSampler) proposes every trial; on ~30% of trials the LLM is shown the CMA-ES proposal, sigma, covariance C, top-5 configs and last 20 trials and returns a full JSON config that REPLACES the proposal (override rate 100% for 27B, 95% for 0.8B); the evaluated config is written back into the Optuna trial and told to CMA-ES, so CMA-ES updates from all trials. Code at the v5-era commit differs from the text in three ways: the mean vector is extracted but never rendered into the prompt; the LLM turn is a deterministic every-round(1/r)-th trial after 10 warm-up trials, not a probability; and LLM call exceptions silently fell back to the CMA-ES config (Section 4, Algorithm 1; centaur_backend.py @ 89f390e).
   - *narrowed*: Text says the LLM receives the mean vector and acts "with probability r"; code at 89f390e renders no mean and uses a deterministic modulo schedule after 10 warm-up trials. Cite the mechanism from the code, not the abstract.
   - anchor `Section 4, after Algorithm 1` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "The LLM may override the proposal, and in practice does so in nearly all cases: 100% of the time with the 27B model and 95% with the 0.8B model."
   - anchor `Section 4, after Algorithm 1` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "Crucially, CMA-ES updates its internal state from all trial results, including those where the LLM overrode its proposal, so the optimizer continuously learns from the full trajectory."
   - anchor `centaur_backend.py:213 (_should_use_llm)` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@89f390efb8f6e925d75f8178df6412627c335078:autoresearch_automl/backends/centaur_backend.py (last commit before v5, 2026-04-17): "interval = max(1, round(1.0 / self._llm_ratio))"
   - anchor `centaur_backend.py (_override_trial_params)` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@89f390efb8f6e925d75f8178df6412627c335078:autoresearch_automl/backends/centaur_backend.py (last commit before v5, 2026-04-17): "storage.set_trial_param(trial_id, hp_name, internal_val, dist)"
6. Centaur results at n=3: 27B 0.9763+-0.0005, 0.8B 0.9766+-0.0008, Gemini 3.1 Pro 0.9767, Opus 4.6 0.9739+-0.0012 (best); cross-seed std falls from 0.0036 (CMA-ES) to 0.0005; LLM trials were 32% of trials and 25% of incumbent improvements (one seed). Ratio ablation (2 seeds): r=0.1 best for 0.8B (0.9744), r=0.5 best for 27B (0.9746), r=0.8 degrades to 0.9849 / 0.9902 (worse than CMA-ES) - but by the code r=0.8 means every post-warm-up trial is an LLM trial (Section 5.3, Table 4, A.6).
   - *narrowed*: The r=0.8 ablation arm is, by the scheduler, 100% LLM after warm-up (round(1/0.8)=1), so "too much LLM control degrades" is really "no CMA-ES turns degrades"; ablation cells are 2 seeds and two 27B cells report std 0.0000.
   - anchor `Section 5.3, end of ratio-ablation paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "This confirms that CMA-ES should retain majority control of the optimization trajectory: the LLM is most effective as an occasional informed perturbation, not as the primary search driver."
   - anchor `Section 5.3, first paragraph` @ arXiv:2603.24647v5 (arxiv.org/html/2603.24647v5, raw sha256 146852873ac9...): "Despite constituting only 30% of trials, LLM trials contributed 25% of incumbent improvements"
7. Authors' post-publication n=8 update (repo blog, revised 2026-06-20): Centaur-Opus 4.6 0.9738 vs TPE 0.9755, one-sided paired Wilcoxon p=0.055; KA (14 HPs)-Opus 4.7 0.9741, p=0.098 (a pure-LLM fixed-space arm second overall); CMA-ES 0.9776, SMAC 0.9816; KA (Code)-Opus 4.6 ties TPE (p=0.53); Centaur-Opus 4.7 loses (0.9760, p=0.84) via override-then-tell poisoning CMA-ES with off-distribution infeasible points under FAILURE_PENALTY=100. The authors call the n=3 headline "thin statistical ice".
   - *unaffected*: Recorded from the authors' own repo blog at HEAD; not peer-reviewed and not in the arXiv versions.
   - anchor `blog TL;DR, first bullet` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@150cd1418b7bd65c3821a844a91598110d3b36b8:docs/blog/2026-05-19-classical-vs-frontier-llms-update.html (raw sha256 46625cbe0ee9...): "Centaur (CMA-ES + LLM) with Claude Opus 4.6 beats TPE at p=0.055."
   - anchor `blog section 4 "Part of the closing is TPE getting better data"` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@150cd1418b7bd65c3821a844a91598110d3b36b8:docs/blog/2026-05-19-classical-vs-frontier-llms-update.html (raw sha256 46625cbe0ee9...): "the paper's headline numbers were on thin statistical ice and we should have noted that more clearly."
   - anchor `blog section 2 "Why Centaur with Opus 4.7 underperforms", loop step 4` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@150cd1418b7bd65c3821a844a91598110d3b36b8:docs/blog/2026-05-19-classical-vs-frontier-llms-update.html (raw sha256 46625cbe0ee9...): "CMA-ES updates its mean and covariance using Y (an off-distribution point) with a 100.0-loss penalty."
   - anchor `blog TL;DR, second bullet` @ github.com/ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@150cd1418b7bd65c3821a844a91598110d3b36b8:docs/blog/2026-05-19-classical-vs-frontier-llms-update.html (raw sha256 46625cbe0ee9...): "Karpathy Agent (14 HPs) with Opus 4.7 is the runner-up across all 12 method × generation cells at p=0.098 marginal-sig vs TPE (n=8), mean 0.9741."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b COMBINED INGEST+DIVE (arXiv:2603.24647v5; ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl@89f390efb8f6 for code, @150cd1418b7b for README/blog). (1) Dispatch premise corrected: current version is v5, not v2. (2) Centaur text-vs-code: no mean vector in prompt; deterministic schedule; silent CMA fallback at the paper commit. (3) r=0.8 ablation = all-LLM after warm-up. (4) "significant" gap has no test; n=3. (5) The authors' n=8 update narrows the fixed-space and code-editing conclusions and diagnoses a Centaur composition pathology (override-then-tell with FAILURE_PENALTY=100). (6) "reliability dominates exploration breadth" is intake-1367#record's paraphrase; not in the source. 2026-09-15 STAGE-2b ROUND-2 CORRECTION (intake-1381#record): (7) this entry surfaced LLMSYS-HPOBench (arXiv:2605.08305) as a multi-objective classical-HPO test on LLM-serving surfaces. The benchmark records many objectives, but its only optimizer comparison is SINGLE-OBJECTIVE (RS/HB/BOHB/SMAC/HEBO at defaults, one objective per system, figure only, no TPE, CMA-ES or LLM arm), so it cannot adjudicate this entry's TPE-vs-CMA-ES or Centaur questions. (8) 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 13] Hansen, Injecting External Solutions Into CMA-ES (arXiv 1110.4181, id recalled not fetched) - none stated, CMA-ES proposer already declined; [row 14] LLAMBO 2402.03921, SLLMBO 2410.20302, HOLLM 2601.13892, LLaMA-ES (ESANN 2024) - earlier LLM-in-HPO designs, none stated; [row 15] krokotsch.eu autoresearch-hyperopt - already read and consumed by this dive; [row 16] ferreirafabio/autoresearch-automl docs/data/trials.json - p-value re-derivation data, none stated.

### Verdict

Use this source for BOOKKEEPING rules on hybrid LLM+sampler loops (encode infeasible configs so the sampler learns them; tell LLM-authored trials back; cap LLM control share; the override-then-tell poisoning risk), not as proof that classical HPO beats LLMs - the n=3 headline is inside noise and the authors' n=8 update makes it model-dependent. It does NOT settle that CMA-ES or Centaur should own autokernel U3: it only supports a classical proposer in a regime of cheap, numerous trials with effects well above noise, which U3 has not reached.
adopt_patterns, not adopt_component: the transferable parts are bookkeeping rules (tell infeasible configs as dominated, tell LLM trials back to the sampler, cap LLM control share, refuse repeats mechanically), not Centaur itself, whose published mechanism overstates its code and whose override-then-tell can poison the optimizer. Credibility 3: arXiv v5 no venue (+0), <=12 months (+1), Hutter/Zela AutoML group (+1), no commercial bias (0), one independent partial corroboration (krokotsch blog, +1).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `autopilot-continuous-optimization.md`
- updated `autokernel-unified-surface-program.md`
- updated `autokernel-rebuild-program.md`

## intake-1373#record — How Far Are We From True Auto-Research?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19156
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. ResearchArena runs three off-the-shelf coding agents (Claude Code/Opus 4.6, Codex/GPT-5.4, Kimi Code/K2.5) through ideation, experiments, writing and self-refinement on 13 CS seeds (5 CPU, 8 GPU) x 3 trials = 117 papers; stages 1-3 each include an agent self-review loop that revises below threshold for up to 3 rounds (Abstract; App. G, v1).
   - *unaffected*: Counts and design verified against Sections 1, 3.1-3.2 and App. G.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Across 13 computer science seeds and 3 trials per agent-domain pair, ResearchArena yields 117 agent-generated papers, each evaluated under three complementary lenses"
   - anchor `Appendix G, Self-refinement effectiveness` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "At each of the three authoring stages (ideation, experiment, paper), the agent self-reviews and revises if the score falls below threshold (up to 3 rounds)."
1. A manuscript-only LLM reviewer (Stanford Agentic Reviewer, SAR) is a weak discriminator: on 200 ICLR-2025 papers it compresses the human accept-vs-reject gap from 1.52 to 0.25 points, and its manually-labelled verdicts accept 76% of human-accepted and 52% of human-rejected papers (Section 5.1, App. H, v1).
   - *unaffected*: Table 3 and Table 14 values verified verbatim; the SAR acceptance labels are the authors' manual reading of SAR prose (App. H), stated as such.
   - anchor `Section 5.1 SAR cannot be trusted in isolation` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "the human accept-vs-reject score gap is 1.52 points (6.54 vs. 5.02), but SAR compresses that gap to only 0.25 points (5.59 vs. 5.34)."
   - anchor `Section 5.1 SAR cannot be trusted in isolation` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "SAR accepts 76% of human-accepted ICLR papers and 52% of human-rejected ones, but only 41% of Claude Code’s, 22% of FARS’s, 13% of Codex’s, and 5% of Kimi Code’s."
2. Artifact-aware peer review (PR) has ALL THREE agents review every paper (351 reviews, so each paper gets one review from its own authoring agent class), with read-only workspace access, checking results integrity against results.json/logs and references by online lookup; every agent's mean score falls below its SAR score. The human lens is two of the authors jointly meta-reviewing each paper - not blinded, not independent of the study (Sections 3.4, 3.5, 5.2, v1).
   - *narrowed*: PR reviewers are the authoring agent classes themselves, not blinded, and PR is never calibrated against human scores; Section 6's 'PR over-credits relative to humans' has no supporting measurement in the paper.
   - anchor `Section 3.4 Artifacts-Aware Peer Review (PR)` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Reviewers check results integrity against experimental artifacts and reference integrity by online lookups against arXiv, Semantic Scholar, and CrossRef. Each reviewer is given read-only access to the workspace;"
   - anchor `Section 5.2 Artifacts-aware peer review surfaces three failure modes` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Under artifacts-aware PR review, every agent’s score drops below its SAR score (Figure 7)"
   - anchor `Section 3.5 Human inspection` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "The authors serve as meta-reviewers. For every paper, two authors jointly assess both the manuscript, experimental artifacts, SAR review, and PR reviews."
3. Manual integrity audit: paper-vs-artifact mismatch / fake references Kimi Code 77%/72%, Claude Code 31%/36%, Codex 5%/8%; underpowered 49.6% and plan/execution mismatch 23.9% overall (Table 4); none of the 117 papers reaches the acceptance bar of a top-tier venue (Section 5.2, Abstract, v1).
   - *narrowed*: 0/117 is a PR/human-lens result. Under PR it is forced by the instrument (acceptance = 8; two reviewers' observed max = 6), and under SAR 8/39 Claude Code papers clear the paper's own SAR >= 6 threshold. Cite as 'no paper cleared artifact-aware review', never as an instrument-independent quality verdict.
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Fabricated results` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Kimi Code shows by far the highest rates (77% paper-vs-artifact mismatch, 72% fake references)"
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Fabricated results` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Claude Code follows at 31%/36% (occasional fabrication when experiments fail; Case 2); Codex stays clean at 5%/8%."
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "None of the 117 agent-generated papers reaches the acceptance bar of a top-tier venue."
   - anchor `Appendix B, Reviewer guideline (stage 4)` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Acceptance threshold is 8. Score 6 triggers a revision loop. Score < 6 is rejected."
4. Self-refinement raises the agent's own review score at the ideation and experiment gates (e.g. Claude Code idea 100% of rounds improved, avg +2.1; experiment 88%, +2.2) but not at paper writing; cross-agent reviewer severity (Codex mean 2.89 vs Kimi Code 5.69) dominates any self-favouring effect (App. G Tables 11-13, v1).
   - *unaffected*: Tables 11-13 verified. The paper does not itself test whether self-refinement gains survive PR; that linkage is EPYC's reading and lives in notes, not in the claim.
   - anchor `Appendix G, Self-refinement effectiveness` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "Self-refinement is effective for ideation and experiment-execution (avg. +2.0 to +3.0 for Claude Code / Kimi Code at those gates), but limited for paper writing, where revising tends to leave scores unchanged or lower them."
   - anchor `Appendix G, Peer-review bias` @ arXiv:2605.19156v1: "the bias is mostly an across-the-board reviewer effect rather than an agent-specific self-favouring effect"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b (s2b-valid): born dive-verified. intake-1367#record's dive-surfaced summary ('0/117 meet a top-tier bar') is correct as quoted but omits that the bar is instrument-forced under PR and contradicted under SAR - see claim_corrections[claim_index 3].
- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b (s2b-valid): the intake-1367#record dive note proposed that 1367 claim 2 (now claim_index 1) acquires a depends_on on this entry once ingested. Counterfactual applied and REJECTED: 1367 claim_index 1 rests on its own 21-system rubric audit and definitions; 1367 cites this paper in Appendix A.5 and Section 7 as a 'different and compatible question'. Retracting this entry would weaken EPYC's reading that L2 is empirically real, not 1367's claim. No edge.
- 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 17] Stanford Agentic Reviewer (paperreview.ai, no arXiv id) - none stated, SAR declined as an instrument; [row 18] arXiv 2604.23593 When AI reviews science (title only, unread) - none stated.

### Verdict

Cite this as measured evidence that manuscript-only LLM review does not separate good from bad work (Table 3) and that giving the reviewer the artifacts changes the verdict. Do NOT cite 0/117 without the instrument caveat, do NOT cite PR as human-calibrated, and do NOT cite it as showing self-review specifically is the failure - Appendix G says reviewer severity dominates self-favouring.
adopt_patterns: adopt the artifact-aware rule (a reviewer that cannot inspect the artifact the claim rests on is annotating, not validating) and the numbers-vs-results.json check; decline the paper-generation harness itself (EPYC produces no papers).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `autopilot-continuous-optimization.md`
- updated `vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

## intake-1374#record — AI scientists produce results without reasoning scientifically

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18805
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Corral evaluates LLM science agents on 8 domains (4 workflow, 1 strategic, 3 hypothesis-driven) with >25,000 runs: 3 models (GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, gpt-oss-120b) x 2 scaffolds (ReAct, structured tool calling), temperature 0.0 for main results (Abstract; Sections 2, 4.3, v1).
   - *unaffected*: Design and run count verified in abstract, Fig. 1B caption and Section 4.3.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "we evaluate LLM-based scientific agents across eight domains, spanning workflow execution to hypothesis-driven inquiry, through more than 25,000 agent runs"
   - anchor `Section 2, The base model determines performance` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "each paired with two scaffold architectures (ReAct and structured tool-calling), across all eight domains and their task scopes"
1. In the best-fitting Bayesian latent-factor model (M7, PSIS-LOO), IRT-estimated reasoning ability explains 41.4% of explained variance in task success, environment x scope 30.1%, scaffold 1.5%, tool verbosity 0.1% (Section 2, Fig. 3C, v1).
   - *reattributed*: The 41.4% belongs to IRT reasoning ability (per model-environment latent) in model M7, not to model identity per se; the abstract's 'base model 41.4%' is a gloss. Cite as 'reasoning-ability term 41.4% vs scaffold 1.5% in the fitted model'.
   - anchor `Section 2, The base model determines performance` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Reasoning ability accounts for 41.4% of the explained variance, environment scope for 30.1%, scaffold for 1.5% and verbosity for 0.1%."
2. Epistemic operations (hypothesis/evidence/test/judgment/update/commitment) and dependency edges are coded by an LLM (Claude Sonnet 4.5, temperature 0.7, two stages, each node/edge carries a supporting quote auto-checked against the cited message), on ReAct traces only; agreement on 25 traces: human-human 92.6% (mean PABAK 0.853), human-LLM 95.7% (Section 4.8, v1).
   - *narrowed*: Agreement stats come from 25 traces, pooled; the coder is the same model as one evaluated agent and same-model bias is not stratified. The rates in claim_index 3 inherit this.
   - anchor `Section 4.8 Epistemological graphs` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Each trace was annotated using Claude 4.5 Sonnet in two stages."
   - anchor `Section 4.8 Epistemological graphs` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Only ReAct traces were used, as they contain explicit reasoning steps."
   - anchor `Section 4.8 Epistemological graphs` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Inter-annotator agreement analysis on a representative sample of 25 traces showed substantial human–human agreement (overall 92.6%, mean PABAK 0.853) and even higher human–LLM agreement (95.7%)"
3. Across coded traces: untested claims 53% (63% in hypothesis-driven domains), evidence non-uptake 68%, beliefs never updated 71%, refutation-driven revision 26%, convergent multi-test evidence 7%; the same topology appears across domains and models (Section 2, v1).
   - *unaffected*: All five rates verified verbatim in Section 2; subject to the coder caveat recorded on claim_index 2.
   - anchor `Section 2, Reasoning breakdowns predominate` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Untested claims, hypotheses stated without experiments designed to test them, appear in 53% of traces overall and 63% in hypothesis-driven domains."
   - anchor `Section 2, Reasoning breakdowns predominate` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "Evidence non-uptake, gathering a result and proceeding without incorporating it, occurs in 68% of traces. In 71% of traces, beliefs are never updated. Only 26% of traces exhibit refutation-driven belief revision. Convergent multi-test evidence, multiple independent lines of evidence brought to be..."
4. No critic or reflection scaffold was tested - only ReAct and structured tool calling. The strongest context intervention (replaying partial successful trajectories) helps workflow tasks after 1-2 steps but helps hypothesis-driven tasks only near-complete, and Pass^k decays below 0.05 by k=4-6 in hypothesis-driven domains (Sections 2, 4.3, 4.9, Fig. 5, v1).
   - *narrowed*: The scaffold-cannot-repair conclusion covers ReAct vs structured tool calling plus trace injection only; no critic, verifier or reflection scaffold was run. It is NOT evidence that a critic pass fails (or succeeds).
   - anchor `Section 4.3 Agent implementations` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "We used two agent scaffolds."
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "They persist even when agents receive near-complete successful reasoning trajectories as context, and the resulting unreliability compounds across repeated trials in epistemically demanding domains."
   - anchor `Section 4.5 Manual trace annotation` @ arXiv:2604.18805v1: "two expert annotators completed the full annotation process on 773 selected traces. Each selected trace received annotations from one domain expert."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b (s2b-valid): born dive-verified. intake-1367#record's dive-surfaced summary said this paper 'measures whether a critic pass changes belief'. OVERTURNED for that framing: no critic pass was tested; the paper measures belief revision inside single-agent ReAct/tool-calling traces.
- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2b (s2b-valid): depends_on counterfactual for intake-1367#record claim_index 1 REJECTED - 1367 cites this work in Appendix A.5 as a complementary diagnosis operating at a different level (individual run reasoning vs loop control structure). Retracting it leaves 1367's audit and taxonomy unchanged.
- 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 20] github.com/lamalab-org/corral (Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.19659851) - SI annotation data only, none stated.

### Verdict

Cite for: LLM agents frequently state untested hypotheses and ignore gathered evidence, measured by an LLM coder validated on 25 traces. Do NOT cite as evidence about critic/verifier passes (none tested), do NOT cite 'base model 41.4%' without the IRT-reasoning attribution, and do NOT treat a planner's mechanism narrative as validated because the A/B beside it passed.
adopt_patterns: adopt two patterns - (1) separate EFFECT claims (gated by executable A/B) from MECHANISM claims (planner narrative, unvalidated) in keep records; (2) any LLM-coded label EPYC relies on carries a small human-agreement sample with a chance-corrected statistic (PABAK), as this paper does. Decline Corral environments (chemistry/materials, no transfer).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `autokernel-unified-surface-program.md`

## intake-1375#record — FML-bench: A Controlled Study of AI Research Agent Strategies from the Perspective of Search Dynamics

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17373
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1367#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. FML-bench is 18 ML research tasks across 10 domains, each an established baseline in a public research codebase; it isolates agent STRATEGY (what to try next, prompts, memory) from shared execution infrastructure (one LLM-call code editor, one execution environment, one metric display) and enforces val/test separation, so the agent only ever sees validation metrics and the best-validated codebase is scored once on a held-out test set (abstract; Section 3.2; Appendix J).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "We propose FML-Bench, a benchmark of 18 fundamental ML research tasks across 10 domains that separates agent strategy from execution infrastructure and defines 12 process-level behavioral metrics."
   - anchor `Section 3.2, Iterative experiment loop` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "Within this loop, only the decision of what to try next is governed by the agent’s own strategy (unlocked icon in Figure 2); codebase modification and experiment execution (locked icons) are framework-controlled infrastructure identical for every agent."
   - anchor `Section 3.2, Shared infrastructure components (3)` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "Framework-enforced val/test separation ensures that the agent observes only validation metrics throughout the search; after the budget is exhausted, the framework evaluates the best-validated codebase on a fully held-out test set whose inputs, outputs, and feedback are never exposed to the agent ..."
1. Headline (6 agents x 18 tasks x 3 rounds x 100 steps = 324 runs, single backbone GPT-5.4): TAS v2 (best-first tree search + LLM journal) mean normalized test improvement 0.193 / pairwise win-rate 58.5%; Autoresearch (greedy hill-climber, keep only on strict validation improvement) 0.192 / 57.4%; AIDE and OpenEvolve middle; TAS v1 and AIRA (UCT-MCTS) bottom (0.132 each). Strategy complexity does not predict rank (abstract; Section 4.2; Table 2).
   - *narrowed*: The win-rates differ between the 6-agent table (TAS v2 58.5%, Autoresearch 57.4%) and the 7-agent table (both 56.2%) because adding AdaptiveSearch changes the opponent pool; quote the table you cite. The 18-task MEAN mixes three unbounded-worst tasks on a per-task scale (Appendix E), so the mean is a within-paper summary, not a calibrated effect size.
   - anchor `Abstract, finding (1)` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "strategy complexity alone does not guarantee strong performance: a simple greedy hill-climber nearly matches the best-performing tree-search agent, both well above the remaining agents;"
   - anchor `Section 4.2, opening paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "Across the 18 research tasks, TAS v2 leads with the highest mean normalized test improvement ( 0.193 ) and pairwise win-rate ( 58.5\% ); Autoresearch nearly matches it ( 0.192 , 57.4\% ); AIDE and OpenEvolve sit in the middle tier; TAS v1 and AIRA rank at the bottom"
2. The six compared strategies are ALL LLM-idea-generating agents (parallel-linear, best-first tree, solution-tree, UCT-MCTS, greedy hill-climb, MAP-Elites island evolution); there is NO random, uniform or non-LLM baseline sampler. The simplest arm is Autoresearch, a greedy single-incumbent hill-climber with LLM idea generation (Section 4.1; Appendix B Table 6).
   - anchor `Section 4.1, Agents` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "We evaluate six AI research agents that span the contemporary strategy space: The AI Scientist v1 [31], The AI Scientist v2 [58], AIDE [21], AIRA [50], Autoresearch [24], and OpenEvolve [44]."
   - anchor `Section 4.1, Protocol` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "All 324 main-experiment runs share a single underlying LLM, GPT-5.4, so that any performance gap reflects agent strategy rather than LLM capability."
   - anchor `Appendix B, Table 6, Autoresearch row` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "Greedy hill-climbing. After every step: keep the change if validation strictly improves, discard it otherwise, or enter a bounded debug loop on crash. The only persistent state is the current incumbent; no tree, no population, no journal."
3. Post-hoc hypothesis: greedy search wins on opportunity-DENSE tasks and stalls on SPARSE ones (Autoresearch ranks 1st on the dense half, 6th of 7 on the sparse half). AdaptiveSearch (greedy until the best-so-far slope over a W=50 window falls below epsilon=0.0005, then irreversible multi-branch round-robin from the top-N Phase-1 codebases) reaches 0.208 / 58.6% vs TAS v2 0.193 / 56.2% and Autoresearch 0.192 / 56.2% in the 7-agent table. Opportunity density is computed from the agents' own runs, and AdaptiveSearch's Phase-2 reach threshold is normalized by a constant taken from the 324 main-experiment runs, so the switching agent is designed and evaluated on the same data; no significance test is reported for the 0.015 margin (Section 4.3; Table 3; Appendices D, G).
   - *narrowed*: Label as the authors do: preliminary support. Opportunity density is measured from the agents' own trajectories and AdaptiveSearch's Phase-2 normalization constant comes from the 324 evaluation runs, so the switching result is in-sample.
   - anchor `Section 1, Finding 2` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "As an initial test, we build a simple agent, AdaptiveSearch, which starts with greedy search and switches to multi-branch exploration upon detecting stagnation. It outperforms both TAS v2 and Autoresearch in mean improvement and win-rate, lending preliminary support to this observation."
   - anchor `Section 4.3, closing paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "A post-hoc partition of the 18 tasks by opportunity density (Appendix G) further supports this conclusion: Autoresearch ranks first on high-density tasks but drops to sixth on low-density tasks, while AdaptiveSearch ranks in the top two on both partitions."
   - anchor `Appendix D, Adaptive search guidance` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "(the maximum exploration reach observed across the 324 main-experiment runs for that task)"
   - anchor `Appendix G, Opportunity density` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "the resulting partition reflects what agents actually find rather than what is a priori available above the baseline."
4. Of 12 process metrics, pooled Spearman over 324 runs finds AUC-over-steps (rho +0.784), first-improvement step (rho -0.291), exploration reach (rho +0.115, p=.039) and effective dimension (rho -0.140, p=.011) significant; exploration uniqueness/diversity (rho +0.012), token cost (rho -0.005) and wall-clock (rho -0.094) are not. The authors note AUC partly overlaps definitionally with final improvement and that no cross-embedding-model check was run for the GraphCodeBERT exploration metrics (abstract; Section 5.2; Table 4; Appendix E).
   - *narrowed*: AUC-over-steps (rho +0.784) partly overlaps definitionally with final improvement (authors' own caveat); reach and effective-dim correlations are small (|rho| 0.115-0.140) and rest on one embedding model with no cross-model check.
   - anchor `Abstract, finding (3)` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "process-level analysis reveals that early convergence and directionally focused exploration are significantly associated with final performance, while solution diversity and compute cost are not."
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Non-significant predictors (1)` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "Exploration Uniqueness shows essentially no correlation ( \rho=+0.012 , p=0.82 ). Combined with the significant positive correlation of Exploration Reach, this indicates that exploration quality is better measured by how far the trajectory travels from the baseline than by how many distinct neigh..."
   - anchor `Appendix E, Choice of code embedding model` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "though we have not conducted a formal cross-embedding-model consistency check."
5. Stated limitations: each agent's native code editor and auxiliary subsystems (literature retrieval, reviewing, VLM plot analysis, multi-seed averaging) were uniformly removed, which may underestimate each agent; six agents are not exhaustive; the fixed 100-step budget may affect rankings (Section 6; Appendix C).
   - anchor `Section 6, limitations` @ arXiv:2605.17373v2: "The main limitations of this work are that, to enable controlled comparison, we uniformly removed each agent’s native code editor and auxiliary subsystems (Appendix C), which may underestimate each agent’s performance under its full original configuration; six agents cover the major search topolo..."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-14 STAGE-2B (s2b-bench): the dive-1367 brief framed FML-bench as the instrument for is our planner search better than a baseline sampler. PARTIAL: it is a controlled instrument for comparing LLM search TOPOLOGIES with the editor held fixed; it contains no random or non-LLM sampler, so it cannot settle LLM-vs-sampler by itself.
- Adversarial pass: three bounded searches found no replication, critique or response; only the paper and restatement aggregators.
- 2026-09-15 ROUND-2 SURFACED-SOURCE DECLINES (Stage-3 gate record): operator-declined at the round-2 gate (selection: all recommended = rows 1,3,10,19,22,32). ROUND2-SURFACED.md rows surfaced by this entry's dive: [row 21] MARS (arXiv 2602.02660) - none stated, arm choice belongs to the costed strategy-ablation design.

### Verdict

Cite FML-bench for: strategy-only isolation as the design rule for comparing search policies, and the measured tie between greedy hill-climbing and the best tree search (0.192 vs 0.193). Do NOT cite it as evidence that an LLM planner beats a random or classical sampler - no such arm exists in it. Do NOT cite AdaptiveSearch as a validated improvement - its margin is untested for significance and it was tuned on the evaluation runs.
adopt_patterns: adopt the strategy-only isolation principle, a greedy/no-critic ablation arm and the cheap process metrics (AUC-over-steps, first-improvement step, valid-step ratio); do not adopt the benchmark itself (A100 ML-research tasks, not kernels) nor the embedding exploration metrics. Credibility 2: arXiv preprint no venue (0), <=12 months (+1), known contributors (Zhengyao Jiang - AIDE author; Anirudh Goyal) (+1), no independent corroboration (0).
